# KPC notes: maps, boundaries, park data, trip planners, bots that build them

**Older notes (Oct 14-19) are in `.claude/notes/archive/mapping-2026-10-14-to-19.md`** (Map Rep Tool Box rules in full, the Am I in the Park / Kit / Info Finder / planner builds, Kentucky access points, 2-fers, trails plan, first answer keys). Read it when you touch one of those tools.

## WHERE THINGS ARE (summary written Oct 25 2026, chat 2)
- **Owner:** chat 2 = boundaries + answer keys (KY / US / Canada / world), data/state-gis.json, trails, outlines, camping DATA (state camps bot + data/campstate*), closure SOURCES, 2-fers, Park Control, trip planners, Am I in the Park, Map Rep, Info Finder, Kit, and every bot that BUILDS park data. Only chat 2 starts key / boundary / trails / camping / access runs (HQ Oct 25).
- **Shared code:** `kpc-bounds.js` (window.KPCB) = every outline the tools draw: KY files (`kyFor`, `landAt`), US (`usKey`, `usOutline`: checked key first, 'Approximate' labels), Canada (`canada` → saved file if any, else `caKey` = the Canada key fetched live by id), world (`worldKey`, `worldByName`), `retired()`. Bump `?v=` on every page when it changes. `kpc-map-tools.js` (KPCTA type-ahead, KPCLayers, KPCPin, KPCLocate, KPCFull, KPCHere), `kpc-padus-layer.js` (PAD-US picture), `kpc-trails-layer.js` (national + state trails + Wild and Scenic Rivers).
- **Tools:** Park Control (public, KY), Park Commander (finder.html, KY), KY Trip Planner, KPC US Trip Planner (kpc-us-planner.js: pins for US / Canada / North America / world tiles, park cards, outlines, access points), Am I in the Park (kpc-in-park), Activation Kit (kpc-kit), Park Info Finder (kpc-park-info), Map Rep Tool Box (kpc-maprep, hidden + password, standalone page, US + Canada, data/state-gis.json via stLive) and Suggest page (hidden).
- **Kentucky data:** kpc-ky-data.json (193 parks, `ap` access points, `off` official maps, `tf` 2-fers), ky-*.geojson outlines (bump the `?v=` in kpc-bounds.js / gis-overlays.js / kpc-planner.js when one changes), data/ky-answer-key.json (Mondays), data/ky-retired.json + kpc_retired_sweep.py (retired parks are swept from every list), 2-fers single source .claude/build/fers/fers.json.
- **Answer keys (strict: same name + same kind near the pin; unsure = check list, never joined; hand decisions in overrides.json):** US `kpc_us_key.py` (PAD-US + FWS + agency layers XS + data/state-gis.json layers `sg<st><letter>` pinned to their address) → data/us-key; Canada `kpc_ca_key.py` (CPCAD + provincial layers + province entries of state-gis.json incl. GeoJSON / shapefile downloads) → data/ca-key (+ refs.json, layers.json); world `kpc_world_key.py` (28 countries; EEA, CAPAD, BfN, Natural England, NatureScot, NRW, IGN, BAFU, MLIT A10 + national A10-10 names) → data/world-key + data/world-bounds shards. Golden cases: tests/run_match_tests.py (the US key refuses to write if one fails).
- **Boundary check:** every key records whether the park's pin is inside its joined land (`far`: no shape / edge / well outside; `swap` = a DIFFERENT same-named place holds the pin). `kpc_boundary_report.py` edits ONE issue (label boundary-check, #14) with US / Canada / world sections. Nightly `kpc-boundary-check.yml` rebuilds 14 US states from the saved outlines (release `boundary-cache`), topping up a saved copy with any newer layer.
- **Other park-data bots:** state links / parkinfo (weekly), new refs + search lists (daily, locations.json), world POTA pins (nightly), Canada + North America pins (nightly), state trails (`kpc_state_trails.py`, STATES incl. CANADA and WSR = Wild and Scenic Rivers from the Forest Service national rivers layer; stops itself at 150 min and saves), state GIS scan (quarterly), state camps (Wed), US access points (`kpc_us_access.py`, monthly 6th: official parking / entrance / gate / kiosk / ramp / trailhead points from data/access-layers.json, tied by area name or by sitting on the park's land → data/access/US-XX.json, Kentucky's `ap` shape), park alerts (KY sources).
- **Storage plan Stage 1 (Oct 25):** every park-data bot saves through `.github/scripts/kpc_save.py` (save_json / save_text): sorted JSON, written ONLY when the content changed, no 'updated' / 'saved' / 'when' inside the files; each bot's change dates go in its own `data/status/<script>.json` (workflows `git add data/status`). data/canada-bounds is kept only for parks joined to provincial maps or downloads; CPCAD-only parks are drawn live.
- **Outside research bot (Brandon runs it elsewhere):** worklists in .claude/build/research/ (round 3 = access points, round 4 = closure sources + official park maps). Add layers by APPENDING (never edit an address; removing a dead one is fine), then run the key / bot for those states.
- **Rules that keep biting:** 'Approximate' / 'Unofficial' labels, never delete outlines; '2-fer' = two overlapping POTA parks only; English-only leading-type-word rule must not be ported to other languages; PAD-US firewall refuses long WHERE clauses; a failed run emails Brandon (workflows never fail; temp probes deleted after); one notes save per session.

## Bot queue (Oct 20)
- **kpc-bot-queue.yml (Brandon Oct 20: 'set the bots up on a schedule to finish and start another run'):** hourly (:23); when no boundary bot (us-key, ca-key, world-key, state-trails, canada-bounds) is queued/running, it dispatches the first job in data/bot-queue.json {until, jobs:[{wf, inputs, why}]} and removes it; never fails; stops after 'until' (2026-10-22T06:01Z = Thu 1:01 AM CDT). To give the bots more work: add jobs to that file. kpc-world-key.yml now runs ONE COUNTRY PER PASS (an error in one never fails the run; errors print as 'World key error' notices). Rerunning without new sources/rules changes nothing.
- Oct 20 sources added: FR IGN Géoplateforme ZNIEFF (patrinat_znieff1:znieff1, znieff2, znieff1_mer; name field nom_site) with Esri France ZNIEFF_2021 as fallback; AU NSW_Dedicated_State_Forests + QLD LandParcelPropertyFramework layers named 'State forest' (pull_find); US XS fnl FL FNAI, cow CO CPW 'Managed Properties' (layer 5), nep NE Game and Parks, arw AR AGFC, nhc NH GRANIT, mid MI DNR project boundaries, ndw ND, vta VT ANR, mdd MD DNR; 'SWA' = wildlife area kind; easement pieces never join; CA Nova Scotia Parks and Protected Areas layer 1 + Manitoba Wildlife Lands; Baltic word-ending rule (LT LV EE); DE/AT rules (name part + same kind + pin inside; exact name when POTA's type label is off).
- **Land Between the Lakes IS being watched now (Brandon Oct 21: 'Land Between the Lakes was one put it on the list').** `lbl_alerts()` in kpc_park_alerts.py reads https://www.landbetweenthelakes.us/alerts/ and found 3 alerts on 22 Oct. HOW IT IS PUBLISHED, so nobody has to work it out again: the alerts are **JetEngine listing fields** — each one a paragraph inside `jet-listing-dynamic-field__content`, usually starting with its date (`<p>10/20/2026 - Energy Lake Road ...</p>`). An undated paragraph only counts if it mentions a closure, reopening, restriction, detour or damage, which keeps the rest of the page out. DEAD ENDS, do not retry: the WordPress API `/wp-json/wp/v2/posts` is **403**; `/alerts/feed/` and `/feed/` return the **homepage HTML**, not RSS; there is no `fs.usda.gov/alerts/lbl/...` unit page under the codes tried. Everything found pins to **US-3792** — `match_parks()` returns a source's own `refs` when it sets them. Public notices and comment periods are marked routine so they do not go out as closures. Run the workflow with `lbl_look` ticked to see what each route returns. Still untouched: KDFWR wildlife areas and the Corps lakes.
- **Three parks retired, Oct 22 2026 (Brandon Oct 21: 'leave them in park control until I till you or the bots notice they are gone' — the reference check noticed):** US-13604 Lake Linville, US-13605 Wood Creek and **US-13607 Boltz** (Brandon only knew about the first two) are off the official POTA list and were removed from kpc-ky-data.json: **195 → 192 parks**. Five more got their official names: Beaver Lake, Kingfisher Lakes, Carpenters Lake, Guist Creek Lake and Bullock Pen are all 'State Fishing Lake' on POTA's list. THE DURABLE FIX that came with it: the 'quiet ones' post and the bot's 'what needs activating?' and 'most activated' answers now cross-check kpc-top25.json against kpc-ky-data.json, so a retired reference drops out by itself even while the POTA figures file still carries it — no hold list needed. data/kpc-post-holds.json still exists for a park that is doubtful but not yet gone.
- **Where a retired park has to be taken out (Oct 22, after US-13604/13605/13607 went):** `kpc-ky-data.json` is NOT the only place. Also `locations.json` (the nationwide search list), `locations-ky.json` (`rows` + `total`), `kpc-guide-pota-data.json` (`parks`, keyed by reference), and the hardcoded count "195 POTA parks" in kpc-new-to-pota.html, kpc-park-control.html, kpc-club.html, kpc-guide-pota.html (its `#gTotal` fallback) and the two build sources .claude/build/parkcontrol/main.html and .claude/build/guides/src/pota-body.html. LEAVE ALONE: `data/new-refs/seen.json` and `data/ky-lands-seen.json` are bot memory — removing a reference there makes the bot announce it again as new. `kpc-top25.json` refreshes itself from POTA, and the suggestion bots cross-check it against kpc-ky-data.json meanwhile.
- **Kentucky Fish and Wildlife IS being watched (Brandon Oct 21 gave the link):** `kdfwr_alerts()` in kpc_park_alerts.py reads https://fw.ky.gov/Alerts/Pages/default.aspx — a SharePoint page where each alert is a heading linking to its own page; no feed exists. Found 12 on 22 Oct (Buckhorn dam ramp, Sympson Lake, AJ Jolly, Laurel River boat ramp, West Point ramp, storm-hit hunting areas…). References are matched by NAME, not pinned, because these name a lake or an area. Regulation and meeting notices are marked routine so they never go out as closures.
- **Corps of Engineers lakes: LOOKED FOR, no alerts page exists (Brandon Oct 21: 'I looked for CoE alerts and all the pages says I am restricted on this server but maybe you can find it').** The Louisville and Nashville districts have no alerts list of the kind KY Parks, KDFWR and the Forest Service publish — their closures come out as NEWS RELEASES, on the district sites (lrl.usace.army.mil / lrn.usace.army.mil, ASP.NET pages that may need an RSS path like /DesktopModules/ArticleCS/RSS.ashx) and on **dvidshub.net**, which carries USACE Louisville releases and has its own API and feeds. That is a different and messier job than the other sources: prose press releases rather than a list, so it needs the text read for a closure and a lake name picked out of it. WORTH KNOWING FIRST: **no Corps lake is a POTA reference in our data** — the POTA parks are the STATE parks on those lakes (Taylorsville, Barren River, Nolin, Rough River, Buckhorn…), which the KY State Parks source already covers, and the KDFWR source just added covers several of the lakes themselves. So the remaining gap is mostly campgrounds and boat ramps, not activation ground. Decide it is worth it before building.
- **State forests: no closures feed exists (Brandon Oct 21 gave the link, 'only see wild fires on forestry').** Checked https://eec.ky.gov/Natural-Resources/Forestry/Pages/default.aspx — the Division of Forestry publishes no alerts or closures list at all, and its state forests page lists the 11 forests with nothing about conditions. TWO THINGS THERE THAT ARE WORTH HAVING INSTEAD, neither built yet:
  1. **The Interactive Fire Response Map, https://kdf.firesponse.com/public/** — reported, active, contained and controlled wildfires, live. The map must be fed by an endpoint; find it (browser Network tab) and an active fire near a park is better news for an activator than any closure notice. This is the real source for the 7 state forest references.
  2. **Kentucky's fire hazard season** (outdoor burning restricted, roughly 15 Feb–30 Apr and 1 Oct–15 Dec): https://eec.ky.gov/Natural-Resources/Forestry/wildland-fire-management/Pages/default.aspx. A standing seasonal rule rather than an alert, so it belongs as a line on the park card or the Kit during those dates, not as a park-alert post. Note we are inside it right now.
- **Nature preserves (20 references) still have no source** — Office of Kentucky Nature Preserves. Not looked at yet; needs its alerts/closures page found the way Brandon found the Fish and Wildlife one.
- **Nature preserves: CHECKED, no source exists (Brandon Oct 21 gave the link, 'nothing').** https://eec.ky.gov/Nature-Preserves/Pages/default.aspx has no alerts, closures or conditions section — only a general News page (press releases, not closures) and a Locations list. Individual preserve pages might carry a condition now and then, but that is 20 pages polled for something rarely posted. DO NOT GO LOOKING AGAIN unless the Office of Kentucky Nature Preserves starts publishing one.
- **WHERE KENTUCKY ALERT COVERAGE STANDS (22 Oct 2026):** watched = state parks + historic sites (parks.ky.gov), national parks (NPS API), Daniel Boone (fs.usda.gov), Land Between the Lakes (its own site), Fish and Wildlife (fw.ky.gov) — roughly **147 of 192 references**. NOT watched because no agency publishes one: **20 nature preserves, 7 state forests** (plus Clyde E Buckley Sanctuary, which is private Audubon land and never will). Corps of Engineers lakes have no alerts page either, only news releases, and no Corps lake is a POTA reference anyway. The only buildable thing left is the Division of Forestry wildfire map (see above), which covers the state forests better than a closures list would.
- **NAME_JOIN rule added Oct 21 (Brandon: 'that might help you refine their rules and searches').** The hand-check pile was **6,850 parks across 91 places**. Looking at it showed a clear pattern: POTA writes the kind of place onto the end of the official name — "Nemuno delta Regional Park" for an area simply called "Nemuno delta", "Mecklenburgisches Elbetal UNESCO Biosphere Reserve" for "Mecklenburgisches Elbetal". `norm()` already strips generic words, so those names DO agree; the only thing keeping them out of the join was the KIND not matching. `kpc_world_key.py` now joins a check-pile park when every "same name, different kind of place" candidate points at ONE official area, and leaves it alone when two different places share a name. MEASURED AGAINST THE SAVED KEYS BEFORE TURNING IT ON: **600 joined, 44 left as ambiguous** — biggest gains AU 92, ES 47, FI 43, FR 41, GB 39, RO 37, IE 28, PT 22. Joins made this way carry the reason "same name (POTA writes the kind into the name)", so they can be found and undone if it turns out wrong. Turn it off with `NAME_JOIN=0`. Hand-checking the rest is ~6,200 parks, so the way through is MORE RULES LIKE THIS, not reading them one at a time.
- **BOUNDARY CHECK built Oct 21 (Brandon: 'check see if the boundaries are where they supposed to be') — FIRST RESULT NEEDS VERIFYING BEFORE ANYONE ACTS ON IT.** kpc_us_key.py now asks, for every joined park, whether the park's own pin falls inside the land it was joined to; `far` = it does not (`edge` ≈ within about a mile, `well outside` = not close), and `counts.pin_outside` / `pin_well_outside` carry it. FIRST RUN (ID, UT, NV, WY): **314 of 841 joins — 37% — have the pin outside**, 65 of them well outside. **DO NOT READ THAT AS 37% WRONG MATCHES.** The flagged joins look correct by name (Dinosaur National Monument → Dinosaur National Monument; Cedar Mesa BLM SRMA → Cedar Mesa; Callaghan BLM HMA → Callaghan). Two innocent explanations, not yet told apart: (1) BLM herd management / wilderness study areas are vast and multi-part, so POTA's single pin may sit outside every piece; (2) **the check may be broken for those layers** — the run log shows the BLM services returning empty acreage, so if they also return no usable geometry then `inside()` says 'outside' for all of them, which would explain why the BLM-heavy states score worst. NEXT SESSION, FIRST: print whether `_g` has rings for a flagged record. If the geometry is missing, fix the fetch and re-measure; only then is the number worth anything. Also: the NAME_JOIN kind-label rule is in **kpc_world_key.py only** — kpc_us_key.py has its own matching and did NOT gain from it (US check pile moved 898→897). Porting that rule to the US script is an easy win still on the table.
- **BOUNDARY CHECK IS NOW A BOT (Brandon Oct 22 1:09 AM: 'Is the a bot you can build and set up to be checking that on github? That way later you got more answers?').** `kpc-boundary-check.yml` runs nightly at 2.37am Central, rebuilds **six states a night** with the check (it shares the `kpc-us-key` concurrency group so the two can never build at once), and `kpc_boundary_report.py` writes the findings into **issue #14**, EDITING the body each time so nobody is emailed. Nothing in the keys is changed by the report; it only reads. A pin-outside join is now sorted three ways — **no outline** (the service gave the land no shape, so the check is blind and the join is NOT in question), **edge** (within about a mile), **well outside** — plus **swap**, where another area with the SAME NAME does contain the pin, which is a wrong join with the right land already identified and the one case that can be fixed automatically. A state measured before those parts existed is marked **stale** and left out of every total rather than counted as zero. SO: the 37% from the first run is NOT in the report and should not be quoted — ID, NV, UT and WY are stale until the nightly job redoes them. Read issue #14 for real numbers.
- **TWO MATCHING RULES FIXED BY THE BOUNDARY CHECK (Oct 22 — this is what the check was for).** Of 1,095 joins tested across CO, AL, AK, AR, AZ, CA, only **4 were really wrong (0.4%)** — the 37% scare was large scattered areas (Kodiak NWR, Lake Mead) and abbreviations (SWA/WMA), not errors. The 4 real ones showed up two faults in `kpc_us_key.py`, both now fixed and both tested:
  1. **A type word at the START of a name is part of the name.** GEN stripped "refuge" from "Refuge Cove State Recreation Site", leaving "cove recreation site", which then matched Settler's Cove State Recreation Site. `norm()` now puts a leading type word back when stripping it leaves almost nothing. "Kodiak National Wildlife Refuge" still strips normally — the type word is at the END there.
  2. **Upper / Lower / North / South / East / West tell two halves of one place apart.** Upper White Oak Lake AND Lower White Oak Lake had both joined the single White Oak Lake State Park. `exact_name()` now refuses a match when one name carries one of those words and the other does not.
  Still to look at: US-11852 Beaver Creek BLM Wild and Scenic River joined to "Eastern Interior Field Office" — an administrative boundary, not the river; probably a Unit_Nm vs Loc_Nm mix-up in the BLM layer.
  WHAT TO WATCH IN ISSUE #14: the "well outside" column mostly catches scattered areas and abbreviations, so it is NOT the error count. The column that matters is **same-name swap** — it was 0 across all six states. If it ever goes above 0, read it.
  COVERAGE: the nightly job now does **14 states a night** (about 11 minutes each against a 300-minute limit), so all 50 are checked in four nights rather than nine. STILL TO DO: the same check does not exist in `kpc_world_key.py` or for Canada — porting it is the way to check "all of them" rather than just the US.
- **PLAN FOR THE REST OF THE WORLD (Brandon Oct 22 3:37 AM: 'So you have a plan to fix rest of the world?').** In order:
  1. **Port the boundary check to `kpc_world_key.py` and the Canada key.** Same three-way split (no outline / edge / well outside) plus the same-name swap. The world script already has `inside()`, so it is mechanical. FIRST because it tells us whether the world's 67% means anything, the way it just showed the US's 77% is sound (4 real errors in 1,095).
  2. **Cross-port the rules each script is missing.** NAME_JOIN (the kind-label rule) exists only in the world script; the US matcher never got it. The Upper/Lower/North/South rule is US-only and applies everywhere. No new data needed for either.
  3. **Extend PIN_JOIN past Japan.** The "nothing found" pile is **13,623 — twice the check pile** — and gets no attention because there is nothing to look at. The rule took Japan 102→182 joined with its check pile 82→2. Many European candidates are the same shape (pin inside exactly one official area, name written differently).
  4. **Transliteration for non-Latin scripts** — Greece, Bulgaria, Korea, Israel, Thailand, Russia. Japan got romaji (`romaji()` via pykakasi) and nowhere else did, so those countries match English POTA names against native-script official names and fail almost completely.
  ⚠️ **THE TRAP, do not get this wrong:** the Oct 22 rule "a type word at the START of a name is part of the name" is ENGLISH-ONLY and must NOT be ported as it stands. English puts the type word last (Kodiak National Wildlife **Refuge**), but French, Spanish, Italian and German put it first (**Parc national** de la Vanoise, **Nationalpark** Eifel). Porting it naively would keep "parc"/"nationalpark" in every name and wreck matching across half of Europe. Keep it to US/CA-English, or make it language-aware first.

- **BRIEF FOR REVIEW (Brandon Oct 22 3:39 AM: 'Make a note about how the bots work, our goals, our mapping tools and our issues with matching and how it has been difficult to get the boundaries I will ask fable to review it quickly and give us recommendations'):** `.claude/notes/boundaries-brief.md` — written to be read COLD by someone with no history here. Covers why boundaries matter (POTA gives a pin, not an outline), the current numbers, how each key bot works, how the matching actually decides, and seven honest difficulties including the three confident-wrong-answer incidents, the English-only trap in the leading-type-word rule, and the fact that we measured the wrong thing for months. Ends with five questions for the reviewer. Keep it current when the numbers or the rules change.

Also since we pulling daily can that send a message that a park was removed to go ahead and make the changes?'):** kpc_new_refs.py (every morning 12:41 UTC) now (1) rebuilds the Finder search lists from its official reads: **kpc_search_lists.py** update() = POTA US-* / KFF-* / US SOTA rows of locations.json (KY ones from kpc-ky-data.json), locations-ky.json = the rows in its box; a program whose read failed is left alone, POTA too if any state failed (locations.json had not changed since Oct 13: ~300 US parks missing); (2) Kentucky RETIRED check: only if US-KY was read and the KY list is ≥150 and ≥90% of ours → add_retired + sweep fix (kpc-ky-data, guides, outlines, app pack, KML/GPX, Top 25, search) + a Discord note ('📍 Retired by POTA') + a comment on the ky-new-lands issue + /tmp/kpc-reprint → the workflow dispatches kpc-guide-pdf.yml AFTER saving; a retired park back on the list comes off ky-retired.json and is flagged in the issue to add back. Tested with a pretend run (one park gone, one new). The sweep now also drops lookups KEYED by a retired number (app pack outlines) and skips data/ky-answer-key.json (its own bot rebuilds it Mondays).
- **NIGHTLY PINS + RETIRED NOTE (Oct 23, Brandon: 'Do what you think is best … France removes 10 parks … easier on boundary bot just to add note to card park is retired until the monthly update?'):** YES, that is the design. World POTA pins (kpc-world-pota.yml) now EVERY NIGHT 06:17 UTC (was monthly), Canada + North America pins (kpc-canada.yml) every night 08:53 UTC (was weekly), US search list every morning (kpc_new_refs.py). A retired park's PIN goes that night; it is written to **data/retired-refs.json** {refs:{ref:{n,d,src}}} by **kpc_retired_refs.py** record() (back on the list = taken out; kept a year) and the cards say 'Retired by POTA … a new activation will not count' (KPCB.retired()/retiredHtml() in kpc-bounds.js; US planner card retNote(), Info Finder, Am I in the Park). The outline keys (us/ca/world) stay MONTHLY and drop them then. Safety: a country that comes back <50% of last night keeps its pins (world), a failed province or short answer keeps all and retires nothing (Canada), 'updated'-only changes are not written (no empty nightly commits), Canada save retries quietly. New parks between rebuilds already get a PAD-US name-search outline labelled Approximate (usOutline fallback), so no nightly outline matching. US parkinfo (card details + activation counts, 15 MB) stays weekly on purpose (repo size). Seeded with US-7969 (Oct 18), US-13604/13605 (Oct 22).
- **Oct 24 evening (Fable, Brandon: 'work on the open list'; his to-do list 2 for maps is in ideas.md 'MAPS TO-DO (Oct 24)'):**
  - **Boundary check now covers Canada + the world.** `kpc_world_key.py` and `kpc_ca_key.py` run the same pin check as the US key whenever they rebuild (world monthly 3rd, Canada monthly 2nd, or by hand): `far` = no shape / edge / well outside, `swap` = another area of the same name holds the pin, counts `pin_outside / pin_well_outside / pin_no_shape / pin_swap`. World + Canada 'swap' only counts a DIFFERENT place (its land must not overlap the joined land): the first GB run called 23 NNR/SSSI pairs 'swaps' because the same site sits in two layers and the thinned NNR ring missed the pin. `kpc_boundary_report.py` writes ONE issue with a section each for the US, Canada and the world (`data/boundary-report.json` keyed by place). World/Canada outlines are coarser (world rings thinned to 60 points), so their 'just outside' column means less: GB 778 of 3,343, mostly edge.
  - **Side words everywhere:** Upper / Lower / North / South / East / West refuse a match in the world key (`_side(cc, s)`, words per language, French 'sur' and Norwegian 'Alta' excluded, Japanese skipped) and the Canada key (English + French). Cost: GB lost 4 part-of-the-park joins ('Mayesbrook Park, South'), CH 1 ('Les Ordons Est' vs 'Les Ordons'). Golden cases in tests/match_cases.json (new group `ca_names`; the runner loads kpc_ca_key.py up to `recs = cload()`).
  - **PIN + NAME WORDS join, every country (the 'extend PIN_JOIN past Japan' step):** after the name rules fail, the pin inside an area whose name AGREES with the park's (same name with a kind word in the way, or one name's words inside the other's) joins when every such holder is one place; big-area kinds (np, aonb, lsg…) or holders wider than ~0.5° never join on shared words alone ('Cape Cornwall' ≠ Cornwall National Landscape). How text 'pin inside + same name' / 'pin inside + shared name words' = Approximate on the site. Measured offline from the saved outlines (gh release `boundary-cache` assets, fetched through `gh api -H 'Accept: application/octet-stream' repos/…/releases/assets/<id>`): GB 3,101 → 3,343 joined (check 1,182 → 947), CH 249 → 263, AT 303 → 278 (AT had been built once with the blunt PIN_JOIN=AT: 35 Vienna city parks joined to the whole Wienerwald Natura site are now refused, correctly). The blunt rule stays JP-only (workflow input pin_join).

  - **US key:** NAME_JOIN ported WITH a pin guard (the one same-named area must hold the pin; a memorial never joins a refuge): measured on the saved keys it touches 3 parks and joins 0 (the Oct 18 review had already cleared them by hand), kept for new parks. **Bug found + fixed:** the Oct 22 'keep a leading type word' rule kept 'The' ('The 400 State Trail' no longer met '400 State Trail' in WI): `NOTNAME` words are never kept; golden case added. **data/state-gis.json is now read by the US key** (60 state layers → XS entries `sg<st><letter>`, kind from the table, skipped when the address is already in XS; a layer that does not answer is skipped). First live run = the next nightly boundary check / monthly key.

Steese NCA + White Mountains NRA also join 'Eastern Interior Field Office' records, but by acreage those records ARE the NCA/NRA (Loc_Nm), so they stay.
  - **Canada answer key hooked into the site** (Brandon's to-do 2): `KPCB.caKey(ref)` reads data/ca-key/refs.json (ref → province, written by kpc_ca_key.py) + CA-XX.json + layers.json (prefix → layer URLs, written on the next live pull; today only `cpcad`) and fetches the joined land by id; `KPCB.canada(ref)` tries the saved canada-bounds file first, then the key. The planner's SHOW OUTLINE goes through KPCB.canada (dashed + 'APPROXIMATE' when far / not a name join); Info Finder, Kit and In-Park get it through worldByName. Prefixed ids like 'Nova Scotia :49' can belong to two NS layers: the JS keeps the record whose name is the joined name. Scripts ?v=20261024a.

- **Oct 26 5:55 AM (Brandon): POTA'S ACTIVATION LOCATION RULES = THE GOLDEN RULES for the 2-fer bot (and every boundary tool).** Activator + ALL equipment inside the park boundary AND on public property; never private land, even land surrounded by the park; boundaries from the official agency sites (POTA maps = guidelines only); a park must be open to the public (seasonal facility closures may still allow access); vehicles parked on public property inside the boundary are fine. A TRAIL or RIVER designated BY ITSELF (not part of a land park with a boundary): station on public property within 30.5 m (100 ft) of the EDGE of the trail or river. MULTI-PARK: an overlapped area where ALL parks' boundaries intersect, and the intersection must ENTIRELY contain the activator and station; a separate log per park. The bot follows them: holes stay holes, official outlines only, trail/river zone 100 ft (measured from our centre lines = the stricter reading; 5 m to spare), a trail with a land outline is tested as land, n-fers need ALL parks on the same ground, plus KPC's 100-yard spot. Chat 2: run the Basic Ownership test listed in section 6.
- **POTA'S OWN RULES = THE TIEBREAKER (Brandon Oct 28 8:22 PM: 'if the POTA rules are not in this guide put them in it... when in doubt see POTA rules: does it fit these rules?').** Quoted from docs.pota.app/docs/rules.html (read Oct 28 2026). WHEN IN DOUBT, a 2-fer / spot must fit THESE; our rules below only apply them.
  - P1 OPEN PARKS: "Activations must be performed from parks in Parks on the Air's designated list, which are also open to the public." "A park is considered open when the public has civil and legal access, or a special permission/permit to the public lands defined by the park boundary map is obtainable." "A park is closed when the governing agency/website clearly says that public access is prohibited or when the park itself ceases to exist."
  - P2 INSIDE + PUBLIC: "The activator and all equipment must be within the park's boundary and on public property." "Use maps provided by Parks on the Air as guidelines only. Refer to official park websites and agencies to find the official boundaries."
  - P3/P4 PRIVATE LAND: "Activators may not trespass on private property to access state/provincial, or federal lands." "Activators may not attempt to activate from private property, even if the private property is adjacent to, or surrounded by, park property."
  - P5 TRAILS + RIVERS: "If a trail system or a river is designated as a Parks on the Air entity by itself (not as parts of a land park having a defined boundary), the activator and the station equipment must be on public property within 30.5 meters (100 feet) from the edge of the trail or river."
  - P6 TWO-FERS: "Such a multi-park activation requires an overlapped area where all activated parks' boundaries intersect." "The intersection must entirely contain the activator and the station equipment." Straddling a line = "not fully within the boundary and ... out of compliance."
  - P7 LAW FIRST: local/federal laws and licence conditions "supersede anything contained in these documents." POTA calls itself "a self-regulated, community-monitored program."
  - P0 (Brandon Oct 28 8:22 PM, same as CLAUDE.md 'OUR JOB'): a POTA park number = a park. We never question whether POTA should list it; we honor it until POTA retires it, and only find its official boundary + apply P1-P7.
  - So OUR TEST (Oct 28 8:15/8:19 PM) = P2 + P6: two official boundaries intersect + the ground is public = 2-fer; private tracts cut out and labelled (P3/P4); a trail/river park with no boundary = 100 ft rule (P5); a corridor park with a defined boundary uses its boundary, not the 100 ft rule (P5's 'not as parts of a land park having a defined boundary').
- **2-FER RULES: FINAL, IN ONE PLACE (HQ, Oct 29 8:15 AM; Brandon: "write them down, anyone it affects make it clear, do not change them"). This block only COLLECTS his Oct 27-29 rulings; it adds nothing new. If anything below seems to disagree with an older note, THIS block wins; ask HQ.**
  - R0 ONLY POTA-NUMBERED PARKS. No POTA number = does not matter to us, period. We never create a park. A POTA number is honored until POTA retires it. POTA's own rules (P0-P7 above) are the tiebreaker.
  - R1 TWO JOBS. Job 1 (the keys) = for every park number (US-xxxx, KFF-xxxx) the RIGHT official boundary, from the right map, in the right place; ownership is settled HERE (national forests = the land the agency owns; private inholdings cut out). Job 2 (the line) = do two POTA parks' official boundaries overlap on public ground? The line does not re-argue ownership.
  - R2 THE METHOD, every pair: (a) who OWNS the ground (parcels, the agency's own land records); (b) do BOTH parks claim it (layered = 2-fer) or is it a HARD boundary (side by side = not); (c) do the official boundaries really overlap there.
  - R3 THE SIMPLE TEST. Overlap (even a few feet) + public ground = 2-fer, the WHOLE overlap. Several PUBLIC owners on the patch = still a 2-fer. A PRIVATE piece is cut out and labelled 'private land'. Owner unknown in a database = look it up; only that piece stays not sure.
  - R4 LAYERED LAND. A federal POTA park + a state POTA park on that federal land (federal owns, state manages) = 2-fer IN THE OVERLAP. A state-OWNED park inside a federal outer / proclamation line = hard boundary, NOT a 2-fer (shown 'next to it'). A state park or WMA on Corps / TVA land that has no POTA number = one park, nothing to pair. Every layered pair is tracked (MASTER-PLAN 8.12).
  - R5 TRAILS. Trail park = public land within 100 ft of the trail. Water (river) trail = within 100 ft of the water's edge.
  - R6 WILD RIVERS. A wild / scenic river corridor's boundary IS the park (tested like any area park, no 100-ft rule). The river centre counts only if the OTHER park's own boundary claims it.
  - R7 WATER ONLY. When the overlap is only water, it is called 'water only' (boat / kayak). Boat rows are KEPT.
  - R8 KEEP EVERYTHING, REVIEW LATER. Nothing is deleted: boat rows, owner info, 'water map weak here', POTA-history hints (a HINT only: people make logging mistakes) all go on the review list. The simple test decides live; it never removes information. Ownership + % water are INFO on each row, never a deciding rule.
  - R9 SIZE (Oct 27, unchanged): 100+ yd across = main list; 50-100 yd = 'small overlap' label; 25-50 yd = 'very tight, could be map error' (members only); under 25 yd = not shown. (Stricter than POTA, on purpose: official maps can be off 10-15 yd.)
  - R10 NOT A 2-FER: a KFF / SOTA reference sharing ground with a POTA park ('Also:'); parks that only TOUCH at a border; a 'same land' join of two different places far apart (wrong join).
  - EXAMPLES, YES: Mammoth Cave NP x Green River Wild River (US-0050 x US-10105, ~6,852 ac incl. the river: Brandon activates it; permanent known answer); Redbird WMA, Pioneer Weapons WMA, Beaver Creek WMA x Daniel Boone NF; Cohutta WMA x Chattahoochee NF (GA); Cherokee WMA x Cherokee NF (TN); the Kentucky Lake shore 3-fer (US-0703 + US-3790 + US-3791, known answer); Fort Heiman (its own tract) x Fort Donelson.
  - EXAMPLES, TRAIL RULE: Channahon SP x I&M Canal State Trail (IL) = test with the 100-ft trail rule, not as two areas.
  - EXAMPLES, NO: Cumberland Falls x Daniel Boone (dropped Oct 27: state land inside the forest line); a WMA on a Corps lake with no POTA number for the lake (one park); Robert Moses (Long Island) x Thousand Islands, Nova Scotia's label-collision rows (wrong joins, 100s of km apart); Bluestone SP joined to the wide lake / WMA record (fix the join first).
- **2-FER RULES FOR THE WHOLE WORLD (HQ + Brandon, Oct 27 4:29 AM: 'we need rules that apply to the rest of the world also'; the bot (kpc_fers.py) and the desk use these everywhere):** (1) THE PARK = WHERE ITS DESIGNATION LEGALLY APPLIES. Two kinds: (a) 'whole-line' designations cover everything inside the line (UK national parks + AONB/National Landscapes, European nature/regional parks, wilderness, wild river corridors, most state/provincial parks) -> use the line; (b) 'agency-land' designations cover only the agency's own land inside an outer line (US national forests/grasslands proclamation lines, FWS approved acquisition boundaries, Corps project lines, any 'purchase area') -> use only the owned/managed parcels (US: Forest Service Basic Ownership, PAD-US; elsewhere the country's land register or protected-area database), never the outer line. (2) PUBLIC + OPEN: the overlap spot must be public land open to the public (POTA); where a whole-line park holds private land (UK, Europe), the spot must be on publicly accessible land (access layer when one exists, else NOT SURE). (3) LAYERING IS THE NORMAL 2-FER: one designation on top of another on the same ground (WMA on national forest land, wilderness or wild river inside a forest, nature reserve inside a national park, owned by one agency and managed by another) = real. Two owners SIDE BY SIDE = touching, not a 2-fer. (4) SIZE (Brandon Oct 27 4:59 AM): 100+ yards across = main list; 50-100 yards = main list with the 'small overlap: tight fit, check carefully' label; 25-50 yards = a separate VERY TIGHT list, members only, labelled 'could be map error: check both official maps on the ground'; under 25 yards = not shown (official maps can be off 10-15 yards). 'Across' = a circle of that size fits inside the overlap in every direction (the bot shrinks the overlap by half the size from every edge), so long thin slivers never pass. (5) RIVERS + TRAILS (Brandon Oct 27 4:33 AM: 'wilderness rivers are not trails it is an overlap'): a river or trail park WITH an official corridor/area boundary (Kentucky Wild Rivers in ky-wild-rivers.geojson, US wild & scenic river corridors, trail corridors) = an AREA park: test it like any other park (rules 1-4: overlap of the corridor polygon and the other park, 50-yard patch, public + open land, corridor tracts are mixed owners so the spot must be on public land, e.g. Forest Service Basic Ownership or a state-owned tract). ONLY a river or trail park that is just a LINE (no official corridor: national historic/scenic trail centre lines, water trails) uses POTA item 5 = public land within 100 ft (30.5 m) of the trail line or river EDGE, inside the other park; water routes use the official shoreline. (6) SOURCES, best first: the managing agency's official boundary > the national protected-area database (US PAD-US, Canada CPCAD, EU Natura 2000/national registers; WDPA/Protected Planet only if its licence allows) > OpenStreetMap (never confirms a 2-fer alone) > POTA pins (never). Proclamation/purchase lines are not land. (7) NOT SURE when official sources cannot settle it: kept out of every guide/list, re-checked when data arrives; never a 'no'. (8) Always POTA only, always 'we think', both notices (CLAUDE.md), nothing public without Brandon's GO. ADDED Oct 27 4:41 AM (Brandon): (9) WATER ONLY: an overlap only on a lake/river surface is listed SEPARATELY, labelled '🚤 Water only: boat or kayak', never in the main list. (10) CLOSED / RESTRICTED: the spot carries a NOTE (seasonal closures, hunting seasons, nesting, 'restricted' access in PAD-US); a permanent no-entry area (sanctuary, dam security zone, PAD-US access Closed) cannot be the spot. (11) 3-FERS AND UP: every park must overlap on the SAME patch (no chains). (12) MISSING BOUNDARY: a pair where one park has no official boundary is NOT confirmed; it goes in a separate 'Missing boundary: check the official map' list with that note (members first), never the main list. (13) KEEP IT CURRENT: retired POTA numbers drop out, new ones are checked; every spot shows when it was last checked + the map dates used, and is re-checked when either park's boundary changes. (14) OWNERSHIP STEP (HQ + Brandon Oct 27 4:45 AM: 'land ownership might be needed to confirm … lots of grey areas we need to line out'): every pair whose two parks have DIFFERENT managing agencies, or where either is an agency-land park (rule 1b), gets an ownership check before it can be confirmed. Each patch has ONE owner; a 2-fer is real only when BOTH designations legally apply to that patch: (a) LAYERED = real: one agency's designation laid over the other's land (wilderness, wild & scenic river, NRA or national monument on forest/BLM land; an 'overlay' wildlife refuge on Reclamation/Corps/BLM land; a WMA or state park run on federal land by agreement or lease); (b) LINE MISMATCH = not real: the lines cross only because one is an outer/acquisition/proclamation line or a map edge (e.g. a refuge's approved boundary drawn over BLM land the refuge does not manage); (c) NOT SURE = the data cannot tell. US data: PAD-US Fee (owner + manager) + PAD-US Designation (overlays) + PAD-US Proclamation (outer lines, never land); Canada: CPCAD; elsewhere the national register. Tribal and military land: only when officially open to the public. PRIVATE LAND BY AGREEMENT (Brandon Oct 27 5:21 AM: 'pota does not allow private land leased to public … unless it has a park number we allow it till it is retired'): private land opened or leased to the public is NOT park land; ONLY exception = that land has its own POTA park number (e.g. a WMA on leased timber land) -> it counts for THAT number only until POTA retires it, never for a forest/refuge/other park around it, so never a 2-fer with them. Chat 8 collects every new GREY AREA it meets into a list for Brandon's ruling; each ruling becomes a numbered rule here.
- Oct 27 (chat 8, research desk): 2-FER GRAY AREAS list started in .claude/build/research/KPC_2fer_GreyAreas_desk.md (8 open cases for Brandon: state wild river over federal land, private WMAs open by agreement, Corps-land double designations, same-outline pairs, approximate trail lines, trail x trail, Restricted access).

## RULE SHEET A: ALL-STATES CLOSURES (chat 2, Oct 27 5:10 AM, RULES FIRST; DRAFT for chat 8 to break, then Brandon rules, then HQ records)
Covers kpc_us_closures.py ('uc-' items), Kentucky's own ('pa-', 'wma-' items) and any later state/federal feed. One closure = one item tied to one POTA park.
1. WHAT COUNTS: (a) WHOLE PARK closed (all entry); (b) PART of a park closed: a named road, gate, campground, day-use area, boat ramp, trail or section, a unit of a WMA; (c) SEASONAL or DATED closures (winter road gates, burn bans that close sites, construction); (d) HUNTING closures: a WMA or area closed to everyone but hunters (quota hunts, managed deer hunts), with dates; (e) emergency closures (flood, fire, storm damage, algae/water-quality closures of a swim area count only as a NOTE). Each item names the park, what is closed (whole / part: which part), from and to dates when the agency gives them, and the agency link.
2. WHAT DOES NOT: standing rules (hunting seasons in general, 'no pets', 'day use only', permit needs, hours) = park info, not a closure; burn bans and fire danger with nothing closed; a notice about a different park with a similar name or outside 40 km; news posts older than 45 days with no end date; anything from a page that blocks robots (never worked around); social media posts; 'restricted access' in PAD-US (that is a 2-fer NOTE, not a closure); closures of private land inside a park.
3. SOURCES, BEST FIRST: the managing agency's own machine-readable feed (ArcGIS status layer, CSV, RSS: CA, FL, MO, NJ, MT, OR, WI, AR, NC, CO today) > the agency's own alerts page (read politely when robots allow) > federal feeds (NPS alerts API with NPS_API_KEY, Recreation.gov RIDB facility status, Forest Service alerts pages, Corps lake pages) > KDFWR / state hunt calendars for hunting closures > never: news sites, forums, Facebook, POTA comments. A source that fails keeps its last items (a failed read never 'reopens' a park).
4. WHEN UNSURE: can't tie the notice to ONE park (two parks share the name, no location) = not shown, listed in data/alerts/us-parks.json 'unmatched' for chat 8; dates unclear = shown with 'dates not given: check the agency page'; open-ended closure (no end date) stays until the agency removes it or 90 days pass, then becomes 'may be out of date: check the agency page' (never silently dropped); a feed that has not answered for 7 days = its items show 'last confirmed <date>'.
5. WHAT PEOPLE SEE: on park cards / Kit / planners / app: '⚠ CLOSURE FROM THE AGENCY: <what> (<from> – <to>)' + the agency link + 'last checked <date>'; partial: '⚠ PART CLOSED: <road / campground / trail>'; hunting: '🦌 Closed to non-hunters <dates> (quota hunt)'; plus the short info line 'ℹ️ Maps and park info are a planning aid, not a guarantee. Always check the official agency map and rules before you go. [About our information]'. HOW LONG SHOWN: from the date the agency posts it (or 7 days before 'from' for dated ones) until 'to' + 1 day; RSS news items 45 days max unless a 'to' date is later; open-ended per item 4. Nothing posts to Facebook/Discord from here (the Wednesday WMA heads-up is chat 5's post and reads the 'wma-' items).
6. EXAMPLES (incl. hard ones): (1) California State Parks status layer: 'Big Basin Redwoods SP: partially open' = PART CLOSED, shown with the agency link. (2) Missouri: only advisories the agency flags to show (ADVISORY_n_SHOW='Y') count; others are park info. (3) Kentucky: Ballard WMA quota hunt = '🦌 Closed to non-hunters' on those dates only (KDFWR Quota Hunts page). (4) HARD: a Florida campground closed but the day-use area open = PART CLOSED (campground), the park stays 'open'. (5) HARD: Arkansas AGFC RSS 'WMA closure' post with no end date, 60 days old = 'may be out of date: check the agency page'. (6) HARD: NC Parks alert 'Lake Norman SP: swim beach closed (algae)' = a NOTE, not a closure. (7) HARD: Colorado CPW drought sheet 'boat ramp closed: low water' = PART CLOSED (ramp), even though the source is a Google Sheet (CPW links it from its own site: judgment call, kept; chat 8 to confirm). (8) HARD: 'Hickory Run' notice in PA when two POTA parks carry 'Hickory' nearby = not tied, listed as unmatched. (9) Forest Service road closure order inside a national forest = PART CLOSED (road), shown on the forest only, never on a WMA that shares the area unless the order names it. (10) Winter gate closure of a state park road every year Dec-Mar = seasonal PART CLOSED with dates, not a standing rule, because the agency publishes it as a closure.
GREY AREAS for Brandon (chat 8 adds more): (G1) is a hunt-only closure a 'closure' for activators who could still activate from a public road edge? (G2) should a whole-park closure hide the park from 'next park' / trip suggestions or only warn? (G3) how long to keep showing an open-ended closure (90 days proposed)?

## RULE SHEET B: NEAR-MISS OUTLINE MATCHING (chat 2, Oct 27 5:15 AM, RULES FIRST; DRAFT for chat 8)
The near-miss list (kpc_near_miss.py, nightly, data/outline-leads/near-miss.json + issue section) proposes an official outline for a POTA park that has none; a person approves before anything joins.
1. WHAT COUNTS AS A MATCH (all must hold): (a) the official area contains the POTA pin, or its edge is within 1 km of it; (b) the names say the same place: the same main name word(s) after dropping kind words (State Park, WMA, Natural Area, Game Land, BLM, 'Grouse Enhanced Mgmt.Sys.'…) and spelling variants (one word vs two: Hardtrigger / Hard Trigger; Sulpher / Sulphur; accents; 'Mt' = Mount; '#24' = the zone numbered 24); (c) the SAME KIND of place (a WMA joins a wildlife area, a state forest a state forest, a natural area a natural area or nature preserve; a numbered zone only its own number); (d) from an official layer (the agency's own, then PAD-US / CPCAD), never OpenStreetMap alone; (e) the outline is the PARK, not something bigger around it (rule: agency-land parks = owned land only).
2. WHAT NEVER COUNTS: kind alone ('same kind of place' with no shared name word) never joins: it is a lead only; administrative units (field offices, ranger districts, forest management units, 'State Forest Area' compartments, counties, watersheds); a bigger place around a site park (a campground, trailhead, dam, boat ramp, airstrip, historic marker stays a PIN: Sheet rule 'sites'); generic shared words ('lake', 'creek', 'conservation area', 'passive conservation areas', 'prairies'); a city / council park when the POTA park is not that park (Brandon's 'city and council parks = never' list); an outline whose name is a different place that happens to contain the pin (Fossil Mountain WSA inside 'King Top'); a proclamation / acquisition line.
3. SOURCES, BEST FIRST: the managing agency's own boundary layer (state-gis.json, agency federal layers) > PAD-US (US) / CPCAD (Canada) / national registers > OpenStreetMap (lead only, labelled 'OpenStreetMap outline (unofficial)', never a confirmed match) > POTA pin (decides nothing).
4. WHEN UNSURE: stays a lead in near-miss.json (never joined); two candidates for one park = chat 8 picks or leaves it; a pin outside the proposed area by more than 1 km = not listed; a lead that a reviewer rejects goes into overrides.json 'no' so it never comes back.
5. WHAT PEOPLE SEE: nothing until approved. Approved = data/us-key/overrides.json (or data/ca-key/overrides.json) {'join': [id or exact name], 'why': 'reviewed: near miss <date> (chat 8)'}; the next key run joins it and the map shows it as an official outline with its agency as the source, plus the short info line. WHO APPROVES: chat 8 (research desk) checks each lead against the agency's own map; Brandon rules on grey areas; chat 2 only applies approved ones.
6. EXAMPLES: (1) MATCH: 'Jordan River Off-Highway Vehicle State Park' (UT) = Utah's 'Jordan River OHV/ Modelport State Park', pin inside, same kind. (2) MATCH: 'Lawrencetown Beach Provincial Park' (NS) = 'Cole Harbour - Lawrencetown Provincial Park'? HARD: a bigger combined park: chat 8 checks that POTA's park is the whole of it, else NO. (3) MATCH (applied as a rule): Michigan 'Bill Rollo Memorial State Game Land' = MDNR 'Bill Rollo Memorial Grouse Enhanced Mgmt.Sys.(GEMS)(SWMA)'. (4) MATCH by number: 'New Brunswick #24 Wildlife Management Area' = NB Wildlife Management Zone 24 (pin inside); HARD: #5 and #6 pins sit in zones 8 and 7: joined by number, flagged for a look. (5) NEVER: 'Lake Superior State Forest' (MI) -> 'Newberry State Forest Area' (a management compartment, kind only). (6) NEVER: 'Edger Waterfowl National Wildlife Management Area' -> 'Barry County Waterfowl Production Area' (shares only 'waterfowl'). (7) NEVER: 'Bond Lake Conservation Area' -> 'TRCA Passive Conservation Areas' (generic name). (8) NEVER: 'Battle of Rivière des Prairies' -> 'Pointe-aux-Prairies Natural Area' (shares only 'prairies'; a historic site is a pin). (9) HARD: 'Labrynth Canyon BLM Wilderness Area' -> BLM 'Labyrinth Canyon' (POTA misspelling, pin inside, same kind) = MATCH by hand (overrides). (10) HARD: 'Trin-Alcove/Three Canyon BLM Wilderness Area' -> 'Labyrinth Canyon' wilderness (the old study area is now part of it) = NO: the POTA park is a piece, joining gives the whole wilderness.
GREY AREAS for Brandon: (G1) when a POTA park is a PIECE of a bigger official area (example 10), show the bigger outline labelled 'part of …' or keep the pin? (G2) combined parks (example 2): join the combined outline or leave as a pin? (G3) a pin 0.5-1 km outside a same-name area: match, or always a person's look?

- **RULE B-ADD (chat 2, Oct 29 1 AM, from chat 8's 'wrong joins solved' + the no-boundary hunt; built, chat 8 grades):** (B9) ALIAS = another name the official layer uses for a park (overrides.json 'alias'): joins ONLY when the POTA pin is inside that record AND both names say the same kind of place; never a sister place (North/South, No 1/No 2), never a 'unit of' taking its parent, never one park number covering several places, never a land trust's or other owner's layer (Horton Grove taken back). (B10) A record joined to 2+ parks by NAME alone stays only with the parks near it: a claim from a pin more than 15 km from the record is dropped when another park's pin is within 2 km (Robert Moses Long Island vs Thousand Islands, NY '(X County)' forests); hand joins never dropped. (B11) 'far: edge' is NOT a reason to drop a join (3,973 good joins carry it: pins on a road or visitor centre). (B12) NEXT (not built): part-level clipping of a multipart record shared by two parks (PAD-US 237576 holds BOTH Robert Moses parks), designation words must agree (NF vs NM vs Memorial, WMD vs WPA, Wild Forest vs State Forest). **(B12 BUILT Oct 29 5 AM, Fable's list):** distances to a multipart record are measured to its nearest PART; a record of <= 6 parts shared by parks whose pins are > 15 km apart and near DIFFERENT parts = each park keeps only its own parts (key field `parts`, the line reads only those; NY county-bracket forests, Robert Moses, Truman Home/Farm, San Juan Island camps); bigger records (a 500-part national forest) are never cut. The line's 'same land' needs both pins in the SAME part (ring), not just the same record; the checker holds a state on any live 'same land' row with pins > 30 km apart (Canada pins too). Canada: 'same land' also needs the same record NAME (two NS layers share the id prefix 'Nova Scotia ': 144 false rows). Designation words: still NEXT. **FAR-SPOT HOLD (Oct 29 7:30 AM, chat 10 + Brandon 'some live 2fers were wrong, correct them'):** a live area / same-land row whose spot is more than 100 km from a STATE-LEVEL park's pin (not national, trail, river, refuge, BLM, marine…) is held NOT SURE ('was' keeps the old status) until B13 is ruled or the key is fixed: 132 held today (NS 61, FL 30, NB 13, ND 6, NC 5, MB 4, NY 4, PA 3, MN 2, OK 2, OR 1, YT 1); kpc_fers_far_hold.py, also run at the end of every pass 5. **FINER OUTLINES (Oct 29, Brandon 7:07 AM 'yes send it'):** Kentucky's 4 state-GIS files re-read at ~5 m (220 outlines, live, KY proof 19/19); data/canada-bounds re-read at ~5 m with all of a park's records merged (2,654 done, 140 kept old: no record answered; folder 16 -> 31 MB; kpc_ca_refine_bounds.py, refine_v 3). **GAINS/LOSSES (Oct 29 6:40 AM, Brandon 'update our boundary gains loss stat'):** since the start of Oct 28 the US key gained 113 outlines, lost 7 (Loxahatchee on purpose), changed 2,238; Canada +28 / -18 (15 Québec regional parks from a failed read: the key now keeps a failed layer from the saved copy), changed 600. Table: .claude/build/research/KPC_Boundary_GainsLosses_desk.md. **(B13 RULED Oct 29 8:07 AM, Brandon: 'if it is a tract that belongs to the park NEVER delete it … it's overlapping another park or not … the overlap is a 2fer'; and 'if it is state WMA on top of federal land it is a 2fer'): NO distance cutting, ever. A piece is removed only when it is proven NOT the park's land (a wrong record join, an id mix-up, or another POTA park's place in a shared record); a real far tract keeps every overlap it has. The 132 far-spot holds are a CHECK list, not a cut: each is looked at on the map, real tracts go live.** **STATE LAND INSIDE A FEDERAL LINE (Brandon Oct 29 8:07 AM, his words: 'It has to be an overlap of 2 parks...if state owned inside a federal park no over lap not a 2fer...if federal claims all the land (national park + state park) 2fer'):** = chat 8's R-SF: LAYERED (federal owns, state manages / designates) = 2-fer; a state-owned parcel inside a federal line = NOT. Built: the line's 'agency land map shows none of it' rows are now DROPPED (were NOT SURE), with a note that an out-of-date map could flip it; KY proof 19/19 (Little Sextons Creek, Kentenia SF, US-10107 x Daniel Boone drop). Held far rows: 'check the parcels; if the maps are correct and they overlap somewhere, yes' (Brandon). OLD PROPOSAL (rejected): far pieces of ONE park's record: chat 8's B13b = start at the piece holding the pin (or nearest), add every piece within 25 km of a kept one, stop; a record holding two parks is split by piece NAMES, never distance; a short checked 'spread-out' list (Tanana Valley, Florida Keys, Dorer, Upper Mississippi, Cherokee) keeps all; when unsure cut and list the park under 'check pieces' (cutting never invents a 2-fer). **(R-CA1 BUILT Oct 29):** Canadian provincial ids = one prefix per LAYER (first layer keeps the old 12 letters, the next '~1', '~2'): two Nova Scotia layers gave record 731 the same id (144 wrong 'same land' rows); full Canada key re-read started.
## MAP DATA RULE SHEET (chat 2, Oct 27 5:30 AM, RULES FIRST; one sheet for every map-data bot chat 2 runs; DRAFT for chat 8 → .claude/build/research/KPC_RuleSheet_Maps_desk.md; Brandon rules the GREY AREAS; HQ records)
SHARED RULES (every bot below): official sources only, best first: the managing agency's own layer/page > the national database (PAD-US, CPCAD, RIDB, NPS API) > OpenStreetMap (shown only as 'OpenStreetMap outline (unofficial)', never confirms anything) > POTA's pin (locates, never decides). Unsure = HOLD / NOT SURE, never a guess. Hidden until Brandon says GO. Never fail, no emails (house rule). Every page that shows the result carries the short info line 'ℹ️ Maps and park info are a planning aid, not a guarantee. Always check the official agency map and rules before you go. [About our information]'; overlap things also the 2-fer notice. CITY + COUNCIL PARKS, historic sites, markers = never chased for outlines (Brandon Oct 20); POINT PARKS (POTA spots with no official outline anywhere: most BLM 'RMA' sites, trailheads, dams, airstrips, hatcheries, boat ramps) stay pins and are counted separately (SCOREBOARD RULE Oct 26). Every run appends what is worth a human look to data/map-observations.json (OBSERVATIONS LOG below).

1. ANSWER KEYS + BOUNDARY CHECK + SECOND-WITNESS SCORE (kpc_us_key.py, kpc_ca_key.py, kpc_world_key.py, kpc_ky_key.js; nightly kpc-boundary-check.yml; kpc_outline_score.py)
- COUNTS: an official area joined to a POTA park when the NAMES say the same place (kind words dropped, spelling variants) AND the kinds agree AND it lies near the pin; or a hand decision in overrides.json ('reviewed'). Agency-land parks (national forests/grasslands) = owned land only. Score each outline: name 0-2 + agency 0-1 + second map 0-2 + pin 0-2: HIGH ≥5, MEDIUM 3-4, LOW ≤2.
- NEVER: a pin alone (ONE exception, boundary review E: in the WORLD key, where names cannot be read, a pin inside the smallest area of the right kind may join; scored LOW and shown approximate); an admin unit (field office, ranger district, management unit); a lake SURFACE for a land park ('water' layers = approximate); a bigger place around a point park; a proclamation line; an outline with a different place's name; OpenStreetMap as 'official'.
- SOURCES: agency layers (state-gis.json, FWS, BLM, NPS, provincial) > PAD-US / CPCAD > the world's national registers > OSM (unofficial label only).
- UNSURE: 'check' (two candidates / kind clash) = listed in the review issue, not joined; a map that does not answer = keep the last good outline (never drop on a failed read); pin far outside + name and a second witness agree = keep the outline, list 'POTA PIN OFF' (never move a correct outline to a wrong pin).
- PEOPLE SEE: the outline with its agency as source; approximate ones dashed and labelled; 'no official boundary' said plainly; the info line.
- EXAMPLES: (1) Michigan 'X State Game Land' = MDNR 'X Grouse Enhanced Mgmt.Sys.' (name rule). (2) 'Hard Trigger HMA' = BLM 'Hardtrigger' (one word vs two). (3) HARD: Fossil Mountain WSA pin inside 'King Top' WSA = NOT joined (different name). (4) HARD: Hank Aaron State Trail pin well outside, name + second map agree = outline kept, 'POTA PIN OFF'. (5) HARD: Elgin School House SP = Nevada 'Elgin Schoolhouse' (one word vs two). (6) Canada saved copy stale (Oct 22): a 'saved' build dropped 169 joins = reverted; rule: a read that comes back short keeps the old files. (7) NEVER: Daniel Boone NF's proclamation line as its outline (owned land only). (8) HARD: two park numbers on one outline (Fort Heiman + Fort Donelson) = SHOWN as 'Same land, two park numbers' (HQ Oct 27 8:10 + 10:35 AM; was 'an answer-key question, not a 2-fer').

1b. BOUNDARY RULES (HQ + Brandon Oct 27 5:16 AM: 'boundaries are crucial to all the work'; these govern every outline we SHOW, Am I in the Park, the planners, Park Control, access ties, 2-fers)
- ALREADY DECIDED: official only; OpenStreetMap = 'OpenStreetMap outline (unofficial)', dashed, never confirms; city / council parks, historic sites, markers never chased; POINT PARKS stay pins and are counted apart (SCOREBOARD RULE); second-witness score on every outline; a failed read keeps the last good outline; POTA pins never move an outline (POTA PIN OFF list instead).
- (B1) AGENCY-LAND PARKS (national forests + grasslands, wildlife refuges, Corps lakes, any purchase / acquisition line): the outline we SHOW and use = the land the agency OWNS or MANAGES (Forest Service Basic Ownership; FWS Interest FEE records, never the approved acquisition boundary; Corps = PAD-US Fee records managed by USACE), never the proclamation / acquisition line (same as 2-fer rule 1). The outer line is drawn only as a faint DASHED line labelled 'Forest boundary line: not all land inside is public' (ruled B-G1), never shaded and never used for 'inside'. HOW + WHEN: the answer keys get an 'owned land' join for these parks (the bot already uses it for 2-fers); the switch is made per state at the next key rebuild AFTER chat 8 has tested this sheet and Brandon has ruled the grey areas (RULES FIRST), Kentucky's Daniel Boone first (kpc-bounds.js draws Forest Service-owned land; the proclamation file stays as the dotted outer line). AM I IN THE PARK on private land inside the outer line says: 'You are inside the outer boundary of <park>, but this spot is not <agency> land (private or another owner). POTA needs public land inside the park: move onto <agency> land.' plus the nearest owned land and the info line; never 'you are in the park'.
- (B2) WHEN OFFICIAL SOURCES DISAGREE, this order wins: (1) a hand-checked decision (overrides.json 'reviewed', chat 8 / Brandon); (2) the MANAGING agency's own legal or official boundary layer; (3) another agency's official layer that names the park (e.g. a state layer for a federal unit); (4) the national database (PAD-US, CPCAD, the country's register); (5) OpenStreetMap (unofficial label only); (6) no outline (pin only, 'no official boundary'). Within one level: the legal / surveyed boundary beats a generalized one; then the higher second-witness score; then the newer source date. Every disagreement bigger than the half / double size test goes in the observations log.
- (B3) REPLACING AN OUTLINE: a bot may swap an outline only when the new one scores AT LEAST as well (second witness) and comes from the same or a better level (B2). A size change to half or double, or a move of the outline away from the old one, is NEVER silent: the old outline stays shown, the change goes in the observations log ('outline size'), and chat 8 approves the swap.
- (B4) HAND-CHECKED MATCHES ('reviewed', overrides.json join / no, boundary-checked.json): never overwritten, dropped or re-matched by a bot. A bot that disagrees writes an observation; only chat 8 / Brandon change a reviewed match.
- (B5) MULTI-PIECE PARKS: every official piece is kept (all records joined, every polygon of a multipart record, islands, detached tracts); holes stay holes (inholdings are not the park); a piece is never dropped for being small or far, but each separate record must pass the name rules on its own; never 'keep only the biggest piece'.
- (B6) WATER: lakes, ponds and rivers inside a park's official outline ARE park (POTA: inside the boundary); land checks know the shoreline (USGS NHD High Resolution in the US, CanVec / the province's hydrography in Canada): Am I in the Park says 'on the water inside <park> (boat or kayak)' when the spot is water; access pins and 2-fer land spots use land only; water-only overlaps go to the '🚤 Water only' list. A layer that is ONLY a lake surface is never a land park's outline (approximate).
- (B7) AGE: every outline keeps its SOURCE, the source's own date (layer edit date / PAD-US version / the agency's map date) and the date we read it; shown as 'outline: <agency>, <date>'. RE-CHECK: the nightly boundary check re-reads layers that changed; every outline is re-read at least every 3 months (monthly key rebuild rotates through); anything not confirmed for 12 months shows 'outline last confirmed <date>: check the official map'; a park whose agency reports a newer edit date than ours is re-read on the next run.
- EXAMPLES: (1) Daniel Boone NF: shown = Forest Service-owned land; a spot on private land inside the proclamation line = the B1 message. (2) Clarks River NWR: FWS fee tracts shown, the approved acquisition boundary dotted only. (3) HARD: Lake Cumberland (Corps): the Corps fee land around the lake shown; the lake surface is park (water), a state park leased from the Corps is layered. (4) HARD (B2): Cumberland Falls: KY State Parks layer (managing agency) beats PAD-US. (5) HARD (B3): a state layer update shrinks a WMA by half = old outline kept, observation logged, chat 8 decides. (6) HARD (B4): Ocmulgee Mounds 'reviewed' join (renamed 2019) stays even if a bot finds a different name. (7) HARD (B5): Land Between the Lakes in two states and many tracts: every piece kept. (8) HARD (B6): Kentucky Lake WMA: the lake parts are 'on the water'; 2-fer spots there are water only unless land qualifies. (9) HARD (B7): a 2019 PAD-US outline never re-read = 'outline last confirmed …: check the official map'.
- RULED (Brandon Oct 27 5:22 AM, via HQ): (B-G1) OUTER / PROCLAMATION LINE = shown ONLY as a faint dashed line labelled 'Forest boundary line: not all land inside is public'; the SHADED owned / managed land is the park, and only that is used by Am I in the Park, the 2-fer bot and every check (B1 updated by this). (B-G2) PRIVATE LAND BY AGREEMENT OR LEASE (easements, leased or 'open to the public' private tracts) is NOT park land (POTA does not allow private land). ONE exception: land that has ITS OWN POTA park number (e.g. a WMA on leased private land) counts for THAT park number only, until POTA retires it; it never counts toward a forest, refuge or any other park around it, so it can never make a 2-fer (Kentucky: Boone Forestlands, Cumberland Forest, University of the Cumberlands WMAs). Also in 2-fer rule 14. (B-G3) A NEW OFFICIAL LINE FOR A HAND-CHECKED MATCH: the hand-checked one stays shown; the new line goes to the observations log; chat 8 re-checks; whichever chat 8 confirms wins; Brandon decides only if chat 8 cannot tell (B4 updated by this).

2. ACCESS POINTS (kpc_us_access.py → data/access/*.json; Kentucky 'ap' in kpc-ky-data.json)
- COUNTS: official points an activator can drive to or launch from: parking, entrances, gates, kiosks, boat ramps/launches, trailheads, check stations; tied to a park BY NAME (the layer says the area) or BY PLACE (on the park's official land or within ~300 m).
- NEVER: a pin-only tie; a statewide layer by name (every boat ramp in a state counts by PLACE only); private access (Utah Walk-In Access, landowner names); restrooms, signs, campsites as 'access'; owner fields read as type ('City Park' is not parking).
- SOURCES: agency point layers (access-layers.json; Parks Canada open data) > PAD-US-near statewide layers by place > nothing from OSM.
- UNSURE: a point 300 m-1 km out = not tied; coded types read through the layer's code list, else the point's name, else left out.
- PEOPLE SEE: '🅿 parking / access (agency)' pins in the Kit, planners, Park Control; 'check the lot is open and public'.
- EXAMPLES: (1) TWRA WMA entrance tied by WMA name. (2) Statewide Utah boat ramps by place only. (3) HARD: Nebraska ramps hidden by an owner field decoded as 'City Park' (fixed: owner fields out of type tests). (4) HARD: Banff federal + Alberta copies cancelling the inside test (fixed: inside ANY record). (5) NEVER: Utah Walk-In Access (private landowners). (6) HARD: a trailhead 600 m outside a small preserve = not tied.

3. CAMPGROUNDS (kpc_fed_camps.py → data/campfed RIDB; kpc_state_camps.py → data/campstate; Kentucky hand-checked kpc-campgrounds.json)
- COUNTS: campgrounds, cabins, lodges, group camps from the agency (RIDB for federal, the state's own GIS layers), with the official page / reservation link; first-come sites when the agency lists them.
- NEVER: private campgrounds, review sites, OSM-only camps, a 'facilities' row that is not a camp/cabin/lodge.
- SOURCES: RIDB / Recreation.gov (federal) > the state's own GIS + reservation system > (later) Forest Service Recreation Opportunities layer for first-come camps (planning-aid rule).
- UNSURE: no readable official layer = the state is listed 'no list yet' (rolling issue body); a camp not tied to a POTA park shows without a park number.
- PEOPLE SEE: the camp list per state/park with 'Book / official page' links + the info line.
- EXAMPLES: (1) RIDB campground with reservable=1 + link. (2) State GIS 'facilities' layer filtered to camps/cabins/lodges only. (3) HARD: a Corps campground inside a WMA = listed under the lake park, not the WMA, unless named. (4) HARD: first-come FS camps missing from RIDB = round 2 (FS layer). (5) NEVER: a KOA next to a state park.

4. HOURS / FEES / FACILITIES (park info: data/parkinfo/*.json fields, kpc-ky-data.json; Kentucky park pages)
- COUNTS: what the agency states on its own page or API (NPS API fees/entrance types; state park pages for KY), with the date read.
- NEVER: third-party listings, reviews, guesses from photos; 'free' when the page says nothing.
- UNSURE: not given = 'not given: check the park page' (never blank-as-free).
- PEOPLE SEE: the fact + 'from the agency, <date>' + the info line (PARK DETAILS section of About our information).
- EXAMPLES: (1) NPS entrance fee types from the NPS API. (2) KY park hours from the park's own page (watcher flags changes for chat 8). (3) HARD: seasonal hours (summer/winter) = both shown with dates. (4) HARD: a WMA with no fee page = 'not given'.

5. OFFICIAL LINKS + MAPS (kpc_link_harvest.py, kpc_map_finder.py, kpc_map_linkcheck.py → data/parkinfo-web.json, data/map-leads.json)
- COUNTS: official park pages and map links from layer URL fields, the NPS API, RIDB agency links (.gov/.mil/.us); hand values (no '*_by' tag) always win; map finder = SUGGESTIONS only.
- NEVER: support-group / commercial sites as the official map (kept as 'group_site'); a link that does not load (map_check must say ok); guessed file names.
- UNSURE: 'map_hold' until every link loads (link check clears it); two links = 'map' (PDF first) + 'map_page'.
- PEOPLE SEE: a map link only when map_check = ok and no hold.
- EXAMPLES: (1) NPS maps.htm added only when it answers. (2) HARD: Mountains to Sea Trail support site = group_site, NC State Trails page = map. (3) HARD: Utah Lake guessed PDF dropped. (4) Bald Mountain held until the link loads. (5) map finder lead 'Park Brochure (PDF)' = suggestion only.

6. CLOSURES = RULE SHEET A above. 7. NEAR-MISS MATCHING = RULE SHEET B above. 8. 2-FERS = the 14 '2-FER RULES FOR THE WHOLE WORLD' + the size tiers (rule 4).

9. PORTAL SCOUT (HQ Oct 27 9:56 AM; Brandon: 'are we using the bots effectively … they seem to miss often'; rule entry 10:15 AM, build after the Kentucky + harvester runs; kpc_portal_scout.py, monthly)
- COUNTS AS A LEAD: a dataset or map file on a state's / province's OFFICIAL open-data catalog or an agency's own site, found by catalog API (ArcGIS Hub /api/search/v1, Socrata catalog API, CKAN package_search) or by reading an agency 'Maps' tab with a headless browser, that (a) matches a keyword (park boundary, state park, wildlife management area, wild river, trail, trailhead, parking, boat ramp, campground, nature preserve, + every keyword added later), (b) is published by the agency or the state's GIS office (the catalog's owner / publisher field), (c) is a map service, GeoJSON / shapefile download or a map PDF the agency page links, and (d) is NOT already read by a bot (state-gis.json, access-layers.json, campstate / trails layers, KYLIVE, parkinfo-web maps).
- NEVER A LEAD: third-party copies (that unofficial storage site, Avenza, Wikipedia, AllTrails, tourism or club sites unless on the per-agency allow list), city / council park portals (Brandon's never list), datasets marked draft / proposed / pending ('lands proposed for protection' = not official), raster-only imagery, anything behind a login.
- SOURCES, best first: the agency's own catalog / site > the state GIS office's catalog > the national ones (data.gov, open.canada.ca) as a cross-check.
- WHEN UNSURE: listed as a lead with 'publisher not confirmed'; never added to a bot's layer list until chat 8 grades it A (opened, agency confirmed) and chat 2 tests it (lead tester / one-state run).
- WHAT PEOPLE SEE: nothing; leads go to data/map-observations.json (kind 'portal lead': catalog, title, publisher, url, keyword, date) and a count in the boundary-check issue body; no email. WHO GRADES: chat 8 (A/B/C); chat 2 adds the A ones to the right list and runs one state first.
- LEARNING: every miss Brandon or chat 8 finds by hand (e.g. the Kentucky Wild Rivers corridors on opengisdata.ky.gov; the Kentucky State Parks maps on the parks.ky.gov Explore tab) becomes a keyword or a page pattern in the scout's list, with a dated note, so it is never missed again.
- EXAMPLES: (1) opengisdata.ky.gov 'Kentucky Wild Rivers (Corridors)' = lead (KY open data, keyword 'wild river') → graded A → KYLIVE. (2) parks.ky.gov Explore tab 'Park Map' PDFs (kentuckytourism.com, allow list) = map leads, read with the headless browser. (3) NEVER: a county parks Hub layer (city / council). (4) NEVER: NS 'lands proposed or pending protection'. (5) HARD: a state GIS office copy of an agency layer = lead with publisher 'state GIS office': chat 8 checks it is kept current before it beats the agency's own.

- **NAME MISMATCHES: ONE CAUSE BEHIND MOST OF THEM (chat 2, Oct 27 10:30 PM; found while doing the Midwest list, applies to every region).** The desk keeps finding parks that 'look missing' because the state layer drops the type word (POTA 'Beall Woods State Park' vs the layer's 'Beall Woods'; Prophetstown Fen, J.E. Roush Lake, Kohler-Andrae, the three SCAs…). The cause is one line in kpc_us_key.py (~568): `ak = a['_k'] if kinds(nm) else set()` — a record's kind is thrown away unless the record's OWN NAME carries a kind word, and `same_words()` then refuses the pair because it needs `ka & kb`. The kind IS known: data/state-gis.json declares it per layer (SGK maps park->sp, wild->wma, forest->sf, preserve->snp) and the layer is one we picked by hand. PROPOSED NARROW FIX (rule-sheet B territory, 'what counts as a match', so NOT built: chat 8 to break it, Brandon to rule): keep the declared kind for STATE-GIS layers only (source 'STAT'), still throwing away a kind that came only from a PAD-US designation code. Expected: most of the Midwest's 30-odd 'mismatches' and a good share of the Southeast's join by themselves, with the second-witness score unchanged as the guard. Chat 8: please try to break this with cases where a hand-picked state layer holds a place of ANOTHER kind (a state park layer that also holds recreation areas, a WMA layer holding fishing-access points).
