## RULE SHEET C: NAME MATCHING WHEN A LAYER DROPS THE TYPE WORD (chat 2, Oct 27 10:45 PM; DRAFT for chat 8 to break, then Brandon rules, then chat 2 builds)
**The problem.** A hand-picked state layer often names a place without its type ('Beall Woods'), while POTA names it in full ('Beall Woods State Park'). kpc_us_key.py line ~568 (`ak = a['_k'] if kinds(nm) else set()`) throws the record's kind away unless the record's OWN NAME carries a kind word, and `same_words()` needs `ka & kb`, so the pair is refused. This is behind most of the desk's 'name mismatches' in every region (IN Prophetstown Fen, J.E. Roush Lake; IL Beall Woods, Ray Norbut; WI Kohler-Andrae; MO the three SCAs; TN 7 natural areas; WI 8 wildlife areas).
**(1) What counts.** A record from a layer we picked BY HAND in data/state-gis.json, whose entry declares `kind` (park->sp, wild->wma, forest->sf, preserve->snp), may use that declared kind as the record's kind when the record's own name has none. Everything else about matching is unchanged (same_words still needs all the short name's words inside the long one, at most 2 extra, the side-word test, the trail test, the second-witness score).
**(2) What does not count.** A kind that came only from a PAD-US designation code (today's guard stays). A layer whose state-gis entry has kind 'land' (a mixed 'public lands' layer) declares nothing, so it keeps today's behaviour. A county or city layer (never a source: house rule). A bare one-word name still needs today's stricter test (6+ letters, same kind).
**(3) Sources, best first.** Unchanged (rule 6): the agency's own boundary > the national database > OpenStreetMap > never POTA pins. This sheet only decides when two NAMES may be called the same place.
**(4) When unsure.** No match: the pair stays in the 'check by hand' list, never joined (rule 7 thinking). If the declared kind would join a park to a record that ANOTHER park in the same state already matches better (exact name, or more words in common), neither is joined.
**(5) What people see.** Nothing new on any page: this only fills outlines that are already expected. The join reason in the review files says 'same name, kind from the layer' so a person can tell it apart from 'same name + same kind'.
**(6) Real examples, measured on our own data tonight (not invented):**
- **343 pairs of POTA parks in one state share a bare name** once type words are stripped (worst: KS 21, OH 21, NC 19, AR 18, OK 17, FL 16). This is the risk the sheet has to survive — but the fix does NOT drop the kind, it SUPPLIES it, so a 'Chickasaw' record in a state-park layer gets 'sp' and can only reach Chickasaw State Park, never Chickasaw State Wildlife Area or Chickasaw NWR (TN has all three).
- **Only 14 of those 343 collide within the SAME kind** nationwide: AR Muddy Creek WMA x Muddy Creek WMA, CA Sacramento NWR x Sacramento River NWR, CO Gunnison SWA x Gunnison River SWA, LA Boeuf WMA x Lake Boeuf WMA, MI Van Buren SP x Van Buren Trail SP, NE two North Platte NWRs, NV Walker Lake SRA x Walker River SRA, NY Trout Lake SF x Trout River SF. Every one is told apart by a word my test stripped (Lake / River / Trail) that `same_words` does NOT strip, so today's matcher already refuses them — but chat 8 should check each.
- **'Lake X' vs 'X Lake'**: `wset` is a word SET, so 'Lake Boeuf' and 'Boeuf Lake' are the same set and would match. That is right for one place with two spellings and wrong for LA's Boeuf WMA vs Lake Boeuf WMA. This is the sharpest edge of the sheet.
- **A park and its own recreation area** (Indiana's 13 lake SRAs, MO Beaver Creek SCA x SRA): different kinds, so the fix does not join them; they need their own layer instead.
**Open questions for chat 8 (please break these).** (a) Do any hand-picked layers MIX kinds (a 'park' layer that also holds recreation areas or historic sites)? I tried to measure it and my prefix->layer lookup came back empty, so the question is still open and matters most. (b) The 14 same-kind collisions: does today's matcher really refuse all of them? (c) 'Lake X' vs 'X Lake' inside one kind. (d) A layer that covers two states (Land Between the Lakes, interstate forests): does the declared kind travel with it? (e) Anything in the Canada key, where the kind words are French (Parc national, Réserve faunique).

- **BRANDON'S RULINGS, Oct 27 11:28-11:34 PM (chat 8's merged list KPC_Rulings_ForBrandon_desk.md):**
  - **THE PRINCIPLE (Brandon): "our job is not to rule should POTA allow it; our job is: is this the park POTA is calling a park."** If an official outline matches POTA's park (name, size, shape, pin) and it has a POTA number, it is a park.
  - **Q2 + SAME LAND, TWO NUMBERS = A 2-FER (CHANGES Oct 27 8:10 / 10:35 AM):** a place carrying two POTA numbers (Fort Heiman + Fort Donelson; a state WMA with its own number on Corps/TVA/Reclamation/NF land) counts for both = 2-fer, still labelled 'same land, two park numbers' so people see why. Rule 14 ownership step still applies (public land, both designations legally on that ground).
  - **Q4 TRAILS + RIVERS (CHANGES Grey 6):** a line park counts within 100 ft of the trail, or for a river/water trail within 100 ft of the water's EDGE (official shoreline, rule 5), AND on public land. Two trail/river parks with two numbers on the same path/river = 2-FER where both tests pass (e.g. 99 ft from the water on public land on a river carrying two trail numbers). Cards still say 'approximate line' where the official line is approximate.
  - **Q7 COUNTY / REGIONAL PARKS:** Brandon thinks POTA shouldn't allow them, but by the principle: if POTA numbers it and an official outline matches, it is a park (outline it). GRANDFATHER: a former state park handed to a county keeps its POTA number = still a park. (Open: does this also replace 'city/council parks = never' in CLAUDE.md WORK STYLE? HQ asked.)
  - **Q13 DISTRICTS (Wetland Management Districts, Adirondack, Pinelands, multi-tract parks):** ONLY the public pieces the government designates under that park number (several tracts, one number), NO private land between them.
  - Open: Q5, Q6, Q8-Q12, Q14-Q16 (HQ asked 'all leans OK?'); Q1 Russia dropped (not top 10), Q3 charity land waits for the UK GO.
  - **CITY PARKS (Brandon 11:36 PM): YES only 'if you 100% know it is a match by pin, shape, size'.** Same for county/regional. No hunting for city layers; when an official layer we already read matches 100%, outline it; otherwise pin only. CLAUDE.md WORK STYLE updated.
  - **THE OTHER QUESTIONS (Brandon 11:37 PM: 'long as POTA is calling it a park'):** chat 8's leans stand, read through the principle: Q5 a kindless name is never matched by words alone (a person checks); Q6 no-copy / unclear licence = skip until the publisher says yes in writing (HQ drafts the emails on a country's GO); Q8 tiny sites (rec sites, airstrips, beach strips) + Q9 hatcheries = pin only UNLESS an official outline is a sure match (POTA numbers it = it is a park; then outline + 2-fer test); Q10 no-entry / buffer zones never count, the open part does; Q11 permit/season land counts with a note; Q12 state trust/school land = yes where the state owns it and POTA numbers it; Q14 Indigenous co-managed = normal parks; Q15 FL water-district sites = only where published as public recreation; Q16 show 'same land, two park numbers' (now a 2-fer) + one duplicate list to POTA by hand. What rule 14 needs there: the Corps OWNS the reservoir land and the state RUNS a park / WMA on it, so a pair 'state park or WMA + Corps recreation area' (or + a federal refuge on the same lake) is layered = real only if we can see the Corps fee land. Our saved PAD-US copies (boundary cache) hold the Corps-MANAGED recreation areas (IA 34 records, MO 14, SD 7, IN 5, IL 2) but NOT the owner of the state-run parks on Corps lakes, so the number of waiting pairs cannot be counted from saved data. - **Oct 28 ~1:40 AM CDT (chat 2, Fable) OFFICIAL-DOCUMENTS BOT built (HQ 1:24 AM):** KPC-Data `.github/scripts/kpc_docs_bot.py` + `.github/workflows/kpc-docs.yml` + `data/docs/lists.json` (agency LIST pages + agency-wide rules pages from chat 8's OFFICIAL_MAPS survey; add a row the day chat 8 finds a list) → `data/docs/<area>.json` (per park: map {url, kind, date, date_how, how}, rules {url, how, status}, agency, runs[] + handover[] hints, needs_browser) + `data/docs/review.md` (counts per area, 12-park samples, handover hints, no-map reasons, the Kentucky FINDER check) + `data/docs/state.json` (resume + resting hosts). RULE SHEET (for chat 8 to break before any card): (1) THE OFFICIAL MAP = a link on the agency's own site (the park's page or the agency's list page) whose text/address says map/brochure/trail guide; PDF first, then an image, then an ArcGIS layer, then a map PAGE; a map page that links a PDF → the PDF is the map and the page is its source. (2) NOT a map: Google/Bing/Apple/MapQuest/OSM links, site maps, campground/lodge/golf/parking/directions/vicinity maps (scored down), anything off the agency's site. (3) SOURCES, best first: the link already on file (parkinfo-web 'map' = harvester + HQ hand lists; Kentucky 'off' = the answer key) → the agency's list page matched by NAME (never a guessed file name) → the park's own page → a browser pass for script-built pages / download buttons. (4) DATE: printed on the map (first page of the PDF, range request ≤ 2 MB; 'Date Published', 'Revised', month + year, © year) → the PDF's own date stamp → the upload folder (YYYY-MM) / a year in the file name / the server's file date → else 'the day we read it'; the method is ALWAYS written. (5) RULES: federal patterns (NPS lawsandpolicies.htm = Superintendent's Compendium; USFS alerts-notices; FWS visit-us/rules-policies; Corps = 36 CFR 327 ecfr.gov) each OPENED before it is kept → a rules/regulations/permits link on the park's page → the agency-wide page in lists.json. (6) WHO RUNS IT: the agency behind the host; 'managed/operated by …' and 'transferred/handed over to the City/County of …' sentences are HINTS for a person, never applied. (7) UNSURE: status says why ('did not open', 'HTTP 403 → needs browser', 'robots.txt says no', 'host resting'); nothing reaches a card (chat 8 grades a sample; cards after Brandon's GO). MANNERS: robots.txt obeyed everywhere (no exemption: these are websites), 1 req / 2 s per host, back-off 429/5xx, three strikes = a day's rest remembered across runs, PDFs by range, UA names the club site, a park checked < 30 days ago is skipped. TEST dispatched 1:33 AM: areas 'US-MO US-FL US-KY', chunks 2, browser yes, then_queue yes (PASS = Missouri ≥ 90% maps ok AND Florida ≥ 20 maps ok AND Kentucky ≥ 50% → the long run starts by itself: queue US-KY, southeast, midwest, northeast, south central, mountain, pacific (+ MI OH UT), Canada east, west; 5 chunks × 55 min per job, 6 more jobs may follow ≈ 32 h at most). Read the result in Actions → 'KPC official documents bot' annotations + data/docs/review.md. Kentucky FINDER check = 25 parks with a hand-checked map, seed hidden: did the bot find the SAME map from the park's page alone.

- Oct 28 ~2 AM (chat 2, Opus; HQ + Brandon OK, chat 8's KPC_RuleSheet_Docs_desk.md): DOCUMENTS BOT RULES v2 (records made by v1 are read again: `ver`). (1) OFFICIAL HOST = the agency's own host, any host the agency's page links to (third parties like Avenza only that way, labelled 'third-party host, linked by the agency'), or the same government (kentuckytourism.com for KY State Parks; ohio.gov assets; NC; MO; FL incl. the water districts; VA; WV; QLD; Parks Victoria; NPS/FWS/USDA/BLM/Corps/Canada); every map records `via`; off-government + unlinked = not a map. (2) Up to 4 candidates are opened, then the PRIMARY is chosen: the map that NAMES the park (first-page text) beats one that does not; a region overview is never primary while another map exists; when two printed dates are known and > 3 years apart the NEWER wins (`why_primary`); others kept as `map_also`. (3) DATES: `date` = a PRINTED date only (published / revised / updated / month + year), or the PDF's own stamp labelled weaker; `posted` = upload folder / file name / server date (when it went online, never called the map date); a © year is kept apart (`copyright`); sfvrsn / rev tails ignored; none = `date: null`, 'date unknown, checked <day>'. (4) HANDOVER sentence → the park STAYS on the card with `card_note` 'May now be run by <new owner>: check before you go.' + `handover_src`. Also: soft 404s (200 + 'page not found' / sign-in; a .pdf address that is not a PDF) = 'opened, wrong thing'; a 'permits' link is not the rules unless it says rules/regulations. The v1 test (40644560688) was CANCELLED ~20 min in (nothing saved) and rerun with v2 at ~2 AM: MO + FL + KY, 3 chunks, then_queue yes. Chat 8's 11 grey areas (D1-D11) go to Brandon in the morning.

## RULE SHEET D: THE CLOSURES SUPER-BOT (chat 10 'bot workshop', Fable, Oct 28 2026 ~2:30 AM CDT; RULES FIRST; DRAFT for chat 8 to break, then Brandon rules, then HQ records, THEN it is built)
WHAT IT IS: one bot in KPC-Data (own script `kpc_closures_bot.py` + `kpc_manners.py`, own workflow, own folder `data/closures/`) that reads EVERY official closures / alerts source we know (every state's agency pages + feeds, NPS alerts API, Forest Service forest orders / alerts pages, Corps lake pages, Parks Canada bulletins, Kentucky's own feeds) and writes ONE closures file per state / province (`data/closures/US-XX.json`, `CA-XX.json`) in the shape the cards already read (the 'uc-' items in kpc-conditions.json), merged by chat 2's existing merge. It INHERITS Rule Sheet A (above; Brandon ruled G1-G13 on Oct 27 8:10 AM: all of chat 8's recommendations) and the Georgia sheet (website.md; GA1-GA4 ruled Oct 28 1:49 AM); this sheet only adds what a super-bot needs. It REPLACES nothing until chat 8 grades a side-by-side (old uc-items vs new) and Brandon says GO; kpc_us_closures.py (chat 2) keeps running until then.
1. WHAT COUNTS AS A CLOSURE (Sheet A rule 1, sharpened): an agency notice that says people may NOT ENTER some or all of a POTA park's land for a stated or open-ended time. Four classes, each its own label:
   (a) WHOLE: the park itself is closed to entry ('X is closed', 'closed to all access', 'closed until further notice').
   (b) PART: a named road, gate, trail, section, campground, day-use area, boat ramp, lake, unit or tract is closed to entry; the rest stays open.
   (c) HUNT: closed to everyone but permitted hunters on dates (quota / managed / drawn hunts; 'closed to all other public use'). Kentucky: KDFWR's quota-hunt calendar and WMA pages; Georgia: WRD's quota hunts (GA4 yes); other states as their agency publishes them.
   (d) SEASONAL: a closure the agency publishes with dates every year (winter road gates, nesting / eagle / bat closures, beach plover closures, fire-season closures of an area). Shown with its dates; chat 8's note: not a standing rule.
   Emergency closures (fire, flood, storm, evacuation) are (a) or (b) with 'as of <date, time>' (G10) and the 30-day stale clock (G3).
2. WHAT DOES NOT COUNT (shown as a NOTE or not at all, never with the ⚠ closure label): standing rules and hours ('day use only', 'closed Mondays', Otter Creek's Wed-Sun hours, 'no pets', 'permit required'); PERMIT-ONLY land = a NOTE '🎫 Permit needed: <agency's words>' (Peabody WMA user permit; Georgia's WMA licence; a drawn permit for a tract) because the land is open to permit holders and POTA allows it (Brandon Q11: permit / season land counts with a note); hunting SEASONS in general ('deer season Nov 14-29: wear orange') = a NOTE '🦌 Hunting season: <dates>'; closed FACILITIES with access open (restroom, visitor centre, water, office) = a NOTE (G5); roads closed to VEHICLES only, walking allowed = a NOTE (GA2 / G4 'closed to vehicles: open on foot', softer); CAUTIONS, advisories, low water, algae swim-area notices = a NOTE (Sheet A ex. 6, GA); burn bans / fire danger with nothing closed; shooting ranges unless the sentence closes the whole park (GA); a lease that ended / 'no longer public' = the note 'may not be public land now' AND a row in the park truth checker's handover list (GA3), never a closure; anything only on social media or news sites (G8); a notice we cannot tie to ONE park (Sheet A rule 4: 'unmatched' list for chat 8); private land inside a park.
3. SOURCES, BEST FIRST (per park, the first that answers wins; two agencies for one park = both shown, never reconciled, G11):
   (1) the managing agency's MACHINE-READABLE feed (ArcGIS status layer, CSV, JSON, RSS: today CA FL MO NJ MT OR WI AR NC CO; the NPS alerts API with NPS_API_KEY; Recreation.gov RIDB facility status for federal campgrounds);
   (2) the agency's own CLOSURES / ALERTS PAGE read politely as text (data/closure-pages.json's 49 pages + georgiawildlife.com/closures + fw.ky.gov + parks.ky.gov + the pages chat 8 adds; a page that blocks robots is listed as 'not readable', never worked around; a script-built page goes to the browser pass like the documents bot);
   (3) FEDERAL by pattern, each opened before it is kept: NPS per-unit alerts (API first, else nps.gov/<unit>/planyourvisit/conditions.htm), Forest Service 'alerts-notices' pages + the FOREST ORDERS (closure orders) list per forest (fs.usda.gov/<forest>/alerts-notices; orders are PDFs: read the first page by range for the order number, the area and the dates), FWS refuge 'alerts' / 'visit-us' pages, Corps lake pages (news only: Sheet A says Corps has no alerts pages), TVA, BLM unit pages;
   (4) Parks Canada per-park 'Important bulletins' pages (parks.canada.ca/pn-np/<prov>/<park>/visit/bulletins; English and French read, English shown, French kept in 'details_fr' for QC / NB per G9) + provincial park alerts pages (Ontario Parks 'alerts' per park, BC Parks 'Advisories', Sépaq, Alberta Parks advisories, Parcs NB) as chat 8 lists them;
   (5) Kentucky's existing readers (kpc_park_alerts.py: parks.ky.gov, fw.ky.gov, Daniel Boone, LBL) stay the Kentucky source of record; the super-bot reads the SAME pages and chat 8 compares the two until they agree, then Kentucky moves over with Brandon's GO.
   A read that fails keeps the last items (Sheet A); an item is removed only after 2 good reads in a row without it, and the card says 'notice taken down: check the agency page', never 'open' (A5).
4. WHEN UNSURE: everything in Sheet A rule 4 + G3 (open-ended: 90 days, emergency 30, named long-term up to a year) + G6 (one closure on several parks: only once ownership says the land is both parks' land, else the named park with 'may also affect nearby parks') + G7 (mass closures: one state banner + per-park items) + G12 (a spot inside a closed part is hidden from the 2-fer lists until the closure ends; the 2-fer bot reads data/closures/). Dates: 'from' and 'to' only when the agency gives them; 'Updated m/d/yy' on a page is the agency's own date (Georgia) and sets the stale clock (GA1: over a year = shown, 'may be out of date'). A page whose layout changed (0 rows read) = keep last items + a line in the check-up, never an email (house rule).
5. WHAT PEOPLE SEE (one wording everywhere: cards, Kit, planners, app, the Wednesday WMA post, the Activators Lounge): the agency's OWN SENTENCE in quotes (A7 / G9), never our paraphrase, prefixed by the class label: '⚠ CLOSED: "<sentence>" (<agency>, <from> – <to> | updated <date>)' · '⚠ PART CLOSED (<road / trail / campground / unit>): "<sentence>"' · '🦌 HUNT: closed to non-hunters <dates>: "<sentence>"' · '📅 SEASONAL: <what> <dates>: "<sentence>"'; notes: 'ℹ️ Note: "<sentence>"', '🎫 Permit needed: "<sentence>"', '🚗 Closed to vehicles, open on foot: "<sentence>"'; then the agency link + 'last checked <date>' (emergency: date + time) + the SHORT info line from notices.md once per card. A whole-park closure WARNS and sorts the park last in 'next park' / trip suggestions, never hides it (G2). Nothing posts from this bot; the Wednesday WMA heads-up (chat 5) reads the Kentucky HUNT + PART items from this file once Kentucky moves over. Canada: the agency's sentence in the language of the page; QC / NB French kept beside the English (G9).
6. EXAMPLES (real pages, read Oct 25-28):
   (1) KENTUCKY WMA HUNT (fw.ky.gov quota hunts; the Wednesday post today): Ballard WMA, Nov 14-21 → '🦌 HUNT: closed to non-hunters Nov 14 – Nov 21: "huntable portion closed to all access except drawn quota hunters"' on US-3796; Peabody WMA Unit 2, one Saturday → '⚠ PART CLOSED (Unit 2): "Part of Peabody WMA (Unit 2) is closed Saturday for a hunt."' (option A, G9) on US-3828.
   (2) KENTUCKY PERMIT (fw.ky.gov Peabody page): 'A Peabody WMA User Permit is required' → '🎫 Permit needed: "…User Permit is required…"', NOT a closure.
   (3) GEORGIA (georgiawildlife.com/closures, read Oct 28): John's Mountain WMA 'Roads 995E and 955B are temporarily closed.' → '⚠ PART CLOSED (roads)'; Paulding Forest WMA 'Hickory Mountain Rd. … closed to vehicle traffic for ongoing roadwork' → '🚗 Closed to vehicles, open on foot' (GA2); Grand Bay WMA boardwalk 'closed until further notice', Updated 10/08/26 → PART CLOSED, open-ended, 90-day clock from 10/08/26; Sparks Cut Off VPA 'lease has not been renewed for 2026' → the note 'may not be public land now' + a handover row (GA3).
   (4) NPS ALERTS API (developer.nps.gov alerts, KY, Oct 2026): Mammoth Cave 'Flint Ridge Road closed for repaving through Nov 15' (category Park Closure) → '⚠ PART CLOSED (Flint Ridge Road)' with the API's 'lastIndexedDate'; a 'Caution' category item ('Expect delays at the visitor center') → '🚗/ℹ️ Note'. HARD: category 'Information' items that say 'closed' in the text ('Green River Ferry closed for high water') → PART CLOSED by the sentence, not the category (the sentence wins; chat 8 to confirm).
   (5) FOREST ORDERS (fs.usda.gov Daniel Boone alerts-notices): 'Order 08-08-01-26-xx Red River Gorge cliff closures for peregrine falcon nesting, Feb 15 – Jul 31' → '📅 SEASONAL: cliff closures Feb 15 – Jul 31' on the forest (US-4484) AND on Clifty Wilderness (US-10111) only if the ownership step says the closed cliffs are inside it (G6; else 'may also affect nearby parks'). HARD: a closure order on a Forest Service road inside Redbird WMA (Forest Service land run by KDFWR) → shown on BOTH the forest and the WMA (A1 fix: tied by land, not by the name in the order).
   (6) PARKS CANADA BULLETINS (parks.canada.ca …/visit/bulletins): Bruce Peninsula 'The Grotto parking requires a reservation; the park is open' → a NOTE; 'Halfway Log Dump trail closed due to erosion' → PART CLOSED; Forillon (QC) 'Secteur Nord fermé / North sector closed' → PART CLOSED with both languages kept.
   (7) STANDING HOURS vs CLOSURE (fw.ky.gov Otter Creek brochure): 'Open Wednesday through Sunday, daylight to dark' → park INFO (hours), never a closure; an added sentence 'closed Dec 1-3 for a youth hunt' → HUNT with dates.
   (8) SERVICES CLOSED (NPS shutdown wording, Oct 2025 pages): 'Visitor center and restrooms closed; park grounds remain open' → NOTE only (G5); 'Park closed to all visitors' → WHOLE.
   (9) MASS CLOSURE (Florida State Parks hurricane page, 2024 wording): 'All state parks in the following counties are closed' → one Florida banner linked to the page + a WHOLE item on each named county's parks that we can tie by county (G7); parks we can't tie → the banner only.
   (10) VANISHING NOTICE (Missouri advisories page, Oct 2026): an advisory present Monday, gone Tuesday, back Wednesday (page reset) → kept through Tuesday (A5: 2 good reads without it), card never says 'open'.
GREY AREAS for Brandon (chat 8 adds more): D1 should HUNT closures on a WMA that POTA also lists as a 2-fer with a forest hide the shared 2-fer spot for those dates (G12 says a closed part hides the spot; a hunt closure closes the whole WMA to non-hunters)? lean yes, with the dates. D2 permit-only land where the permit is a DRAWN permit (quota) for the whole season (not a buyable one): a NOTE or a HUNT closure for non-drawn people? lean HUNT (closed to everyone else). D3 Forest orders that close an area to CAMPING or FIRES only (not entry): a NOTE 'no camping / no fires' (lean yes, a note) or a PART CLOSED? D4 the stale clock for SEASONAL items the agency never takes down (a 2019 plover notice still on the page): show with the dates of THIS year only when the page says 'annually / each year'; otherwise 'may be out of date'? D5 when the super-bot and Kentucky's own reader disagree on a Kentucky item during the side-by-side, which shows? lean: Kentucky's own reader (source of record) until Brandon's GO. D6 Canada French-first pages where no English exists (Sépaq): show French with a 'translate' hint? lean yes (the 🌐 translate button is on every page). D7 closures the AGENCY posts only as a PDF list (Illinois, some forest orders): read by range like maps, lean yes. D8 Recreation.gov facility 'closed' status for a campground inside a park that is open: PART CLOSED (campground) on the park card (Sheet A ex. 4) and the campground card: lean yes, both.
STOP: nothing is built from this sheet until chat 8 breaks it (→ `.claude/build/research/KPC_RuleSheet_ClosuresSuper_desk.md`) and Brandon rules; HQ records the final sheet here.

## RULE SHEET E: CAMP & ACTIVATE (which campground is in which park) (chat 10 'bot workshop', Fable, Oct 28 2026 ~3:45 AM CDT; RULES FIRST; DRAFT for chat 8 to break, then Brandon rules, then HQ records, THEN it is built; HQ job 5)
WHAT IT IS: a bot in KPC-Data (own script `kpc_camp_activate.py`, own folder `data/campsites/`) that takes job 4's campground records (data/campinfo, every public campground we know: RIDB federal, state, Kentucky's hand list, Parks Canada), our OFFICIAL park outlines (US + CA keys, Kentucky layers, owned-land masks) and the ruled 2-fer patches (data/fers), and writes per state: campgrounds INSIDE each POTA park, campgrounds inside a 2-FER PATCH ('camp here, activate both'), campgrounds within 100 ft of a LINE park on public land, and NEARBY campgrounds (outside, within ~5 miles) kept apart. For the Activators Lounge campgrounds tab and the campground cards (chat 3), after Brandon's GO. It inherits the 14 world 2-fer rules + the Oct 27 rulings (the park = where the designation legally applies on owned / managed public land; outer lines are never land; same land, two numbers = shown as a 2-fer with that label) and both notices.
1. WHAT COUNTS AS 'INSIDE':
   (a) the campground's POINT (job 4's official coordinate, else the agency's point, never a geocoded street address: data/campstate-manual rows placed 'by the US Census geocoder' are NEARBY at best until a real point exists) lies inside the park's OWNED / MANAGED outline (rule 1: Forest Service Basic Ownership for national forests, PAD-US fee for NPS / FWS / Corps, the state layer for state parks and WMAs; never a proclamation or acquisition line); AND
   (b) the point is more than 15 m (G-M3) inside the edge; 0–15 m = 'at the edge: check the official map'; AND
   (c) the land is public and open (PAD-US access Open or Restricted-with-a-note; a campground on private land inside a park, a concession on leased private land, or a 'closed' access area is NOT inside — it is 'inside the line, not on park land').
   A campground run by ANOTHER body on the park's land still counts (Otter Creek campground run by Meade County on county land that carries the POTA number; a KOA on Forest Service land under permit = inside the forest if the land is Forest Service owned: rule 14a layered).
2. 'CAMP HERE, ACTIVATE BOTH' = the campground point is inside a RULED 2-fer PATCH (main list or 'small overlap' tier only; never the 'very tight' tier, never NOT SURE, never missing-boundary pairs) — by the patch polygon, not by 'inside both outlines' (the patch already carries the ownership step). Water-only patches never (a campground is on land). A campground inside park A and within the 50-yard patch of A×B gets BOTH labels: 'inside A' and 'camp here, activate A + B'. The 2-fer notice (short form) goes on every such row; 'same land, two park numbers' keeps that label.
3. LINE PARKS (trails, rivers, parkways with a line only): a campground counts for the line park when its point is within 100 ft (30.5 m, POTA item 5) of the official trail line, or of the river's EDGE (USGS NHD shoreline), AND on public land (rule 2 / Q4); label 'within 100 ft of <trail / river>'. Corridor parks (an official corridor polygon: wild rivers, W&S river corridors, parkway owned corridors) are AREA parks and use rule 1. 'Approximate line' parks (Pine Mountain today) = NOT SURE, kept out of the lists with the note 'line not official yet'.
4. 'NEARBY' = outside every park outline, within 5 miles (8 km) straight-line of the park's outline edge (or of the pin when the park has no outline), kept in a SEPARATE list under the park, sorted by distance, labelled 'nearby: outside the park, <x> mi from the edge' with the short info line; a nearby campground is never counted toward an activation. Parks with no outline (pin only): no 'inside' rows at all (Rule 12 spirit: missing boundary = not confirmed) — only 'nearby' rows, and the row says 'no official boundary: we cannot say if it is inside'.
5. WHAT PEOPLE SEE (Lounge tab + campground cards + park cards, after GO): per park: '⛺ Campgrounds in the park: <name> (<agency>, <sites> sites, <booking link>)'; per 2-fer: '⛺ Camp here, activate both: <name> — inside the <A> × <B> overlap (we think) ⚠ Parks' boundaries may overlap here, but we can't confirm it. Check both official boundary maps and make your own call before you count it.'; line parks: '⛺ <name>: within 100 ft of the <trail> (public land)'; nearby: '📍 Nearby (outside the park): <name>, <x> mi'; every list carries 'ℹ️ Maps and park info are a planning aid, not a guarantee. Always check the official agency map and rules before you go. [About our information]' once, 'last checked <date>' and the outline source label ('official' / 'approximate' / 'OpenStreetMap outline (unofficial)'); 'inside' rows on an APPROXIMATE outline say 'approximate outline: check the official map'. Closures from the closures file (Sheet D) show on the row (a closed campground = PART CLOSED on the park, the campground row says it). Nothing posts; never 'you can activate from your site' as a promise — always 'we think' + the notice.
6. WHEN UNSURE: a point within 15 m of an edge, on an approximate outline, inside a very-tight patch, on a line park with an approximate line, or on land PAD-US calls private / unknown → the row goes to a 'check first' list (members, with the reason), never to the main lists. Two campgrounds with one name 2 miles apart (RIDB loop vs state record) = one row, both sources, the RIDB point wins for federal, the state agency's point for state. A campground whose park (job 4's 'park' field) disagrees with the outline test → the outline wins and the disagreement goes to chat 8's list.
7. EXAMPLES (Kentucky first):
   (1) ZILPO CAMPGROUND (Forest Service, Cave Run Lake): inside Daniel Boone NF owned land (US-4484) AND inside Pioneer Weapons WMA (US-10158, 'Forest Service land managed by KDFWR', kept 2-fer, Oct 27) → '⛺ Camp here, activate both: Zilpo — inside the Daniel Boone × Pioneer Weapons overlap (we think)' + the 2-fer notice; booking = Recreation.gov. The HARD part: Twin Knobs campground across the lake is the same case only if its point falls in the Twin Knobs 2-fer patch; if it is 60 m outside the patch it is 'inside Daniel Boone' only.
   (2) OTTER CREEK CAMPGROUND (Meade County since July 2026): inside Otter Creek ORA (US-8144, transferred park keeps its outline) → 'inside', run by Meade County; the Otter Creek × Lewis and Clark NHT (US-4572) 2-fer is on the Ohio River BANK, the campground is inland → NOT 'activate both'; the row says 'inside Otter Creek; the river-bank overlap with US-4572 is 0.6 mi away'.
   (3) HOUCHIN FERRY CAMPGROUND (Mammoth Cave NP): inside Mammoth Cave (US-0050) and inside the Green River W&S corridor polygon (US-10105, a corridor = AREA park) where the patch is ruled → 'camp here, activate both' if the ruled patch covers the campground point; else 'inside Mammoth Cave' + 'within 100 ft of the Green River (public land)' is NOT used (the river is a corridor park here, rule 3 second sentence).
   (4) SHELTOWEE TRACE (US-3794-style trail rows; line park with an official line): a Forest Service campground whose point is 80 ft from the trail line on Forest Service land → 'within 100 ft of the Sheltowee Trace'; a campground 400 ft from the trail → not counted (even though the trail passes through the campground loop road: the POINT rule; chat 8: should a campground POLYGON count when the trail crosses it? grey E1).
   (5) BLUE HERON CAMPGROUND (Big South Fork NRRA, KY): inside US-0686 owned land → 'inside'; the Big South Fork W&S river (US-10107) corridor: ruled 'dropped' for the park pair on Oct 27 → no 'activate both' row even if geometry overlaps (ruled pairs beat geometry).
   (6) CUMBERLAND FALLS SRP campground: inside US-1252; the Cumberland Falls × Daniel Boone pair was DROPPED (Oct 27 ruling) → 'inside Cumberland Falls' only.
   (7) A KOA 1.2 miles from Carter Caves SRP: not in our data (private) → never listed; a state park campground 3 miles from a WMA → 'nearby' under the WMA, 'inside' under the state park.
   (8) HARD: a campground placed by a street-address geocoder (campstate-manual 'point: street address') whose address point falls inside the park → still NEARBY-ONLY with 'location approximate', until job 4 or a member confirms the real point (Sheet 10 idea: field confirmation).
   (9) HARD: Land Between the Lakes (US-3792) campgrounds (Forest Service on TVA-origin land) + Kentucky Lake WMA (US-10140, 'owned by TVA') → 'activate both' only where the ruled patch says so; the Corps / TVA shore rulings (PF-3) decide the ground.
   (10) HARD, Canada: a Parks Canada campground inside a national park whose outline is the CPCAD polygon with no owner text (PF-4 NOT SURE) → 'inside' (one park, no ownership question) is fine; 'activate both' with a provincial park next door = NOT SURE until CPCAD / the province settles the owner.
GREY AREAS for Brandon (chat 8 adds more): E1 campground POLYGONS (RIDB has loops, not boundaries): point only (lean yes, point; the trail-crossing case = 'check first' list). E2 group camps, horse camps, backcountry sites, hike-in shelters, cabins and lodges: all 'campgrounds' for this list (lean: yes, each with its kind; day-use shelters out). E3 the 5-mile 'nearby' ring: straight-line from the edge (lean yes; road miles later). E4 dispersed camping areas (a Forest Service 'dispersed camping allowed' zone, no point): a park-level note 'dispersed camping allowed (agency rules)', never a row (lean yes). E5 a campground inside a HUNT-closed WMA for the dates (Sheet D class c): hide the 'activate both' row for the dates, keep 'inside' with the closure (lean yes). E6 should the Lounge sort 'activate both' campgrounds first nationally (a members' trophy list) or only within a state (lean: within a state, plus a 'top 50 US' list after GO)?
STOP: nothing is built from this sheet until chat 8 breaks it (→ `.claude/build/research/KPC_RuleSheet_CampActivate_desk.md`) and Brandon rules; HQ records the final sheet here. Job 4 (the campground INFO bot, facts only) runs regardless.
- **Oct 28 ~4 AM CDT (chat 10 'bot workshop', Fable): FOUR WORKSHOP BOTS in KPC-Data, all review lists / facts, none touches a key, card, the 2-fer line or another bot.** Shared manners module `.github/scripts/kpc_manners.py` (robots obeyed, ArcGIS REST API excepted; 1 req / 2 s per host; back-off; three strikes = a day's rest; UA names the club site). (1) PARK TRUTH CHECKER `kpc_truth_bot.py` + `kpc-truth.yml` → `data/truth/<area>.json`, `review.md`, `checklist-<area>.csv` (second-witness score 0-8, weakest first): agency acreage vs our outline (15% rule, both numbers + source line), handover hints (page sentences, agency newsrooms in `data/truth/news-sources.json`, land-record manager vs POTA type, POTA retired list). WMA (3,966 vs 2,430), Green River SNA (304 vs 440), Mammoth Cave (71,228 vs 52,830), Big South Fork NRA (151,973 vs 125,000). ## RULE SHEET F: THE WAITING ROOM (chat 10, Opus, Oct 28 2026 ~9:50 PM CDT; RULES FIRST; from chat 11's KPC_WaitingRoom_Checks_desk.md + HQ's 12:38 PM item; DRAFT for the 1:20 AM Fable review, then chat 8 breaks it, then Brandon rules; NOT switched on)
Why (Brandon Oct 28): "the bots pass themselves all the time, so the checker bot needs to be built right." One checker looks at every CHANGED data file before it goes live. Code: KPC-Data .github/scripts/kpc_waiting_room.py; known answers data/waiting/known/; replay data/waiting/replay.json.
- **F-1 WHAT WAITS:** outlines (us-key / ca-key), 2-fers, access / parking points + leads, campgrounds (federal, state, campground facts), trails, map / rules links (docs), hours / fees / permits, park sizes (truth), the layer intake list, the POTA park list (retirements). A bot writes its changed file to data/waiting/in/<live path>; the checker runs right after each save (and once a night as a sweep).
- **F-2 THE CHECKS (chat 11's numbers, tuned after a month):** access: any point 100+ km from its park pin (line parks exempt), points down 40%+, points at 0,0 / outside the state; campgrounds: count down 30%+, pins outside the state, one id in two state files, the hand-checked KY Recreation.gov camps within 2 km; campground facts: RV 10-100 ft, sites 1-1,000, the same site count on 3+ camps of one agency, toll-free reservation numbers as a camp phone, 'million / renovation' fees; trails: a line keeps half its points and its length within -25% / +50%; docs: one map/rules page on 20+ parks (or 10% of a state), fire-news / map-pin / social hosts, 3%+ re-tested links dead; hours: the same line on 20+ parks, fees over $500 or in millions, hours without a time, headings ending ':'; truth: 3+ parks OFF on one number from one page, 5+ OFF rows sharing one size, over half the sized parks OFF; outlines: 1%+ lost, a retired park still outlined; intake: a read error must be RETRY, READY needs a licence (not a credit line); 2-fers: candidates or NOT SURE change 30%+, a pass missing; POTA list: 1% or 5+ parks gone = second read; any file saying READY with rows 'not checked / no answer'.
- **F-3 NEVER WAITS:** closures (safety, time-limited: rows that break a reject rule are taken out: ended, another state named, fee / hours / standing notes, results-table rows, duplicates; an EMPTY or garbled file keeps the old one live, stamped 'as of'), weather / EU warnings, spots, band alerts, and a NEW park's pin + name (only its outline / 2-fers wait).
- **F-4 WHEN UNSURE = HOLD:** an unknown kind of file, a check that cannot run (no park list, no network for the link re-test), any checker error = HOLD, never pass. The checker's own failure = nothing is promoted, everything waits, one line to chat 11; red / email only when it has not run for a whole day.
- **F-5 WHAT PEOPLE SEE:** PASS = the new file goes live AT ONCE (no fixed delay; the replaced copy kept in data/waiting/previous/ for UNDO). HOLD = the live file stays exactly as it was; ONE 'please look' line in KPC-Data data/status/inbox-checkers.md: '[bot] [file] held: [check] [old -> new]'; chat 11 looks; nobody looks = live stays as it was. A held file never blocks another file. No 'what changed' summaries (EMAIL rule).
- **F-6 BUILT RIGHT:** (a) INDEPENDENT: its own code, reads only the bot's output + the live copy + known answers + other bots' data, never a bot's own gate / READY / test field; (b) UNDO: 'put back the last good copy' for one file, one bot, or one bot + one area (by-hand workflow); RELEASE: a person approves a held file; (c) KNOWN ANSWERS: every flaw chat 8 / chat 11 finds becomes a permanent check in data/waiting/known/<bot>.json; (d) REPLAY TEST before it is switched on: the real bad outputs from git history must ALL hold and the good outputs must ALL pass (Oct 28: 20/20 = 16 bad incl. WV 1.4 million x76, 83 WV parks on one rules page, VA inciweb maps, 675/980-ft 'RV lengths', the 877 phone, '$5 Million Renovation', CA-2156, the 178 far access points, round-8 closures junk, intake SCDNR/Czech; 4 good: KY 2-fers, Canada ON key, KY federal camps, NY access after the fix); a new flaw = a new replay case.
- **F-7 EXAMPLES:** (1) the 178 far access points (NY 47, WA 19, OK 15…) = HOLD F-A1, the cards keep the old points; (2) GA + SC federal camps sharing 11 ids = HOLD F-C3; (3) WV truth file with 1,400,000 ac on 76 WMAs = HOLD F-R1 + known answer; (4) round-8 KY closures = LIVE with 'Youth under 16 - Free', Bolar Mountain (Bath County, VA) and the results rows taken out; (5) a new POTA park US-99999 = its pin + name live at once, its outline waits; (6) a file kind with no check yet = HOLD 'no check written'; (7) today's live KY hours file = HOLD F-H3 (32 of 107 hours lines 'open year-round' with no time): GREY AREA for Brandon (is 'open year-round' park hours?); (8) a docs file when the runner has no network = HOLD 'unsure: link re-test not run'.
- **GREY AREAS (for chat 8 / Brandon):** G1 'open year-round' as hours (example 7); G2 the 2-fer known answers need a key that survives an outline change; G3 20 re-tested links per docs file enough?; G4 closures: take bad rows out (as built) or hold the whole file when many rows are bad?

## RULE SHEET G: ONE BOUNDARY PIPELINE (chat 2, Fable, Oct 29 2026 12:10 PM CDT; RULES FIRST; Brandon 12:03 PM 'maps need to be accurate and the process smooth and efficient … how do we fix it?' → 12:05 'Yes' to the plan; DRAFT for chat 8 to break, then Brandon rules the grey areas, then HQ records, THEN chat 2 builds)
Why: today a wrong piece is caught five steps late (key → 2-fer line → hold → parcel check → three chats), names alone decide what is drawn, outlines are stored sharp but drawn rough, and every rerun is by hand. This sheet moves every check to the KEY (where the outline is made) and leaves the tools downstream nothing to guess.

**1. What counts as a park's outline (a piece joins the park only if ALL hold):**
- R-G1 OFFICIAL: the record comes from the managing agency's own map (or the federal/national layer for federal units; PAD-US only where the agency has no map). Never OSM, never a drawn guess.
- R-G2 NAME AGREES: the record's name is the park's name, an agreed alias (overrides.json), or the park's name with the layer's type word dropped (rule sheet C). The DESIGNATION words must agree (a Provincial Park never joins a Conservation Reserve record; a Wildlife Refuge never joins a National Forest).
- R-G3 LOCATION PROVES IT (the new rule, replaces 'name is enough'): the POTA pin is INSIDE the record, OR within 100 m of its edge, OR the record touches / lies within 2 km of a piece of the same park that already passed. A piece more than 2 km from every passed piece joins ONLY when the agency's own record carries the park's name AND a unit/tract field that ties it to the park (e.g. 'Daniel Boone NF, Redbird District'); otherwise it goes to the queue, not the map.
- R-G4 WATER: a lake-surface record joins only right at the pin (as today).
- R-G5 SPLIT PARKS: a park the agency splits into named units (Whitewater WMA: Main Branch Unit …) takes every unit whose name starts with the park's name within 30 km, once one unit holds the pin (alias_all, as today).

**2. What does not:**
- a same-named record far away with nothing tying it to the park (Little Sextons Creek WMA's 'far piece', the Lost Lakes of Washington);
- a record that is only PART of the park when the park has its own record (Beall Woods Nature Preserve inside Beall Woods SP joins as a PIECE and is labelled so, never as the whole);
- a bigger record that merely contains the pin (Coeur d'Alene Lake for Mica Bay Boater Park; a 298,000-ac BLM record for four small POTA parks; the Ouachita National Wildlife Refuge for the Ouachita National Forest);
- ONE record for TWO POTA numbers (Kaskaskia + West Okaw units of Shelbyville): both get 'pin only' until unit lines exist; shared records never make a 'same land' pair by accident;
- land POTA has retired (data/retired-refs.json): out of every file, read from that one list.

**3. Sources, best first:** (1) the managing agency's own layer on the state/provincial open-data portal; (2) the agency's ArcGIS Online account; (3) the national layer (NPS legal boundary, USFS, FWS realty, BLM, CPCAD); (4) PAD-US / a zip download from the state clearing house; (5) nothing → PIN ONLY. A layer that needs a login or token is dropped (Alberta trails) and its parks fall to the next source.

**4. What happens when unsure:** the piece does NOT go on the map. It goes to the WAITING ROOM with the evidence on ONE line (park, record name, pin distance, acres, owner, map link) and a yes/no question. One person answers once; the answer lands in overrides.json ('join' / 'no' / 'alias' with 'when' + 'why') and is never asked again. No chat-to-chat lists. The 110 held far rows + the 60 name-match 'not sure' rows are the first batch through it.

**5. What people see:** every outline carries ONE grade saved with it, read by every tool (2-fer line, Am I in the Park, cards, kit, app packs): SURE = official record(s), pin inside, every piece passed R-G1..G5 · APPROXIMATE = a person picked the closest official land (bigger / other shape) or a piece joined by alias within 100 m · PIN ONLY = no outline. Cards say 'Official outline', 'Approximate outline', or 'Pin only (no official outline yet)'. The site draws EXACTLY the stored outline when zoomed in (no second rounding); a simplified copy is used only zoomed out. The short information notice stays on every map (notices.md).

**6. The loop (smooth + efficient):**
- Monthly (the 5th, after the POTA list refresh): per layer ONE tiny request ('changed since we saved?': edit date / feature count / ETag). Unchanged = reuse the saved outline, no download. Changed = re-read that layer, re-grade only its parks, re-run only their 2-fer area. Forced full read once a quarter.
- One 'look at this' line per CHANGED area only (what joined, dropped, acres ±10%, grade changed, and why: 'agency edited its map'); unchanged areas = one line 'no change'.
- No temporary workflows, no hand dispatches except 'full' by Brandon's word. Red run = one email, only when a save failed or a layer broke twice.
- Acreage: exact union acres for every park; the big ones (> 60k points) are done one or two a night by a small job, never inside the rebuild.

**7. Examples (incl. the hard ones):**
1. Daniel Boone NF × Little Sextons Creek WMA: the WMA record's far piece 200 km off = R-G3 fails → queue, not map; the overlap on the WMA's own land near its pin stays a 2-fer (Brandon B13).
2. Florida Keys NWR 235 pieces, Kodiak NWR 249, Payette NF 172: the pieces touch / chain within 2 km of a passed piece → ALL stay (never cut by distance alone; Brandon: 'NEVER delete').
3. Fort Heiman tract (KY) of Fort Donelson: own feature with the unit field 'Fort Donelson National Battlefield tract' → joins US-3790; Fort Donelson keeps all 8 → 'same land, two park numbers' by the hand list, never by accident.
4. Pfeiffer Big Sur SP × Los Padres NF: both SURE; PAD-US fee owner at the patch = Forest Service → layered → R4 2-fer. Julia Pfeiffer Burns / Limekiln: mixed owner → NOT SURE until the line decides.
5. Beall Woods SP: own record missing in the layer; 'Beall Woods Nature Preserve' (275 ac, pin inside) joins as a PIECE → APPROXIMATE, card says so.
6. Saddle Mountain SNA (OR): the state layer says 'Saddle Mountain', pin inside → alias → SURE.
7. Ouachita NF: the bot offered the Ouachita National WILDLIFE REFUGE (pin 13 m from its edge) → R-G2 designation words disagree → no.
8. Kaskaskia + West Okaw (IL): one 6,256-ac record for both POTA numbers → both PIN ONLY until the agency gives unit lines (not a fake 'same land' pair).
9. Bonnechere PP / Cedar Creek (ON): joined a Conservation Reserve / CA record of the same name → R-G2 → no; the park's own record or pin only.
10. Hog Lake (WA, US-12426): state layer 'Hog Lake' within 3 km, pin inside → alias → SURE. Lost Lake (WA, 40 of them): lake record joins only at the pin (R-G4).

**R-G6 FINE LINES (Brandon Oct 29 12:36 PM: 'the finer lines we started using today needs to be a rule, for best accuracy'):** every outline any bot or tool SAVES is stored at the official map's own detail, simplified by at most 3 m (never more than 5 m), with ALL of the park's official records merged into one shape (no record left out, no copy counted twice). Never coarser to save space (keep big files gzipped instead). Tools DRAW the stored line exactly when zoomed in; a rougher copy is only for zoomed-out views. Applies to KY files, Canada, the US key outlines, world outlines, trails and app park packs; a bot that saves coarser is a bug.
- Oct 29 12:40 PM (Brandon 12:39 'note that too'): the US + Canada keys MATCH parks on a rough working copy of the maps (0.01 deg, edges up to ~1 km off) to keep the monthly read fast; that copy is NEVER saved as an outline or drawn. Every test made on it (rule G-1 pin within 100 m, the 2 km chain) adds that much slack before holding a piece, so a real piece is never held because of the rough copy. Saved + drawn outlines follow R-G6 (3 m). Fine-lines check the same day: KY Corps lands (15 m), SOTA land (20 m) and state trails (30 m) savers set to 3 m; one batched rerun of the three still to do.
**GREY AREAS for chat 8 / Brandon:** (a) the 2 km chain distance for far pieces (too tight for Alaska refuges? too loose for city parks?); (b) should a PIECE-only join (Beall Woods) be APPROXIMATE or its own grade 'PART'; (c) the monthly date: the 5th vs right after POTA's own list refresh; (d) who answers the waiting-room questions (chat 8 only, or Brandon on a weekly list); (e) layers with no stated terms: HQ said use (Oct 29 11:27 AM) — record it here as final?
**Build order after the rulings:** (1) key: R-G1..G5 + the grade saved per park (US, Canada, world); (2) the queue lines into the waiting room + overrides as the one decision file; (3) site draws the stored outline (chat 3: kpc-bounds.js / the finder); (4) retired list read by every file; (5) monthly change-check loop; (6) nightly big-park acreage job. Then the far-hold / parcel-check / alias-check bots are retired.
- Oct 29 12:14 PM (Brandon, RULE SHEET G grey area 1): 'Pins can be wrong...this is offical': a piece whose agency's OWN official record names/ids the park joins at ANY distance and does not need the POTA pin inside; a pin outside its official outline never blocks it (goes on the 'POTA pin may be wrong' list). The 2 km chain only for name matches from OTHER maps. G-2..5 = HQ answers (3 grades + 'part of the park' note; monthly check on the 1st; chat 11 answers, chat 2 rechecks; no-terms layers settled).
- Oct 29 12:18 PM (Brandon) RULE SHEET G FINAL: G-2 'official map wins' (piece-only outline = drawn as the agency has it, graded Official, no extra grade/note); G-3 monthly check on the 1st; G-4 chat 11 answers, chat 2 rechecks; G-5 no-terms layers settled = use.
- Oct 29 12:20 PM (Brandon): RULE SHEET G is followed AS WRITTEN (chain of command, rules, order) + his 5 rulings; NO changes by any chat (HQ included) without Brandon.

**R-G7 PIN INSIDE A BIGGER AREA WITH A DIFFERENT NAME (Brandon Oct 29 1:07 PM, added to Rule Sheet G; the 313 state parks + any park like them). Official maps, boundaries, ownership, boundary in the right spot. Every chat follows these steps in this order; no one invents a step:**
1. WHAT IT IS: the official map has one big shape (e.g. 'State Forest and Wildlife Area') and the park's pin sits inside it, but the shape's name is not the park's. The big shape is NEVER used as that park's outline (R-G2). The park stays a pin until a step below places it.
2. THE AGENCY'S OWN RECORD FIRST: search ALL of the managing agency's layers and records for a piece that NAMES or IDs that park (a separate record, another layer, a named unit inside the big shape). Found = that piece is the outline, at any distance, pin inside or not (Brandon 12:14: pins can be wrong). Chat 2's key does this by itself.
3. THE OFFICIAL MAP OF THAT PARK: nothing named in the layers → chat 8 (look-up bot for pages a chat cannot open) finds the park's own official map: the agency's park / boundary / brochure map, its property or deed records, or the county parcel map naming the agency as owner. State open-data portal first, then the agency's park page, then search (RESEARCH ORDER).
4. BOUNDARY, THEN OWNERSHIP: (a) the park has its own boundary line on an official map → that line is the outline. (b) no boundary line anywhere → the outline = the land the park's AGENCY OWNS inside the big shape, from the official parcel / ownership map (same ownership step as the 2-fer rules). (c) neither → pin only, on the waiting-room list with the evidence.
5. PUT IT IN THE RIGHT SPOT: chat 2 traces / cuts the park's piece out of the big shape so it follows the official line (R-G6: 3 m, all records merged). The rest of the big shape is untouched and keeps its own park.
6. CHECK BEFORE ADDING (G-4): chat 11 answers yes/no against the official map (right place, right size, right owner, same park), chat 2 rechecks, then it is added STAGED (freeze: mass dump). Unsure = pin + waiting room, asked once.
7. GRADE (G-2): traced from the agency's boundary map = Official; cut by ownership parcels = Approximate; neither = Pin only. A POTA pin outside the traced line never moves the line: the park goes on the 'POTA pin may be wrong' list.
8. WHAT IS LEFT after steps 2-5 (no named record, no boundary map, no ownership map) is the ONLY part that goes to Fable, after chat 8 reruns its missing-boundaries list on the new key.
9. ANY OTHER QUESTION a chat thinks of while doing this = stop, write it as a numbered grey area under R-G7 for Brandon, keep the park a pin; do not guess.

- R-G7 GREY AREA G-ID2 (chat 2, Oct 29 2:30 PM, for Brandon): a POTA park that IS an easement on private land: Buena Vista SWA (Fishing Easement), CO, US-12189: the agency's own page is titled 'Buena Vista SWA (Fishing Easement)', 41 ac, and the state record of that name (45 ac) holds the pin. Our key drops 'easement' records (private land with public access, Oct 20). Draw the easement's outline as the park (it IS what POTA numbered), or keep the pin only? Pin until Brandon rules.
  - RULED Oct 29 2:22 PM (Brandon): 'We are not here to correct POTA parks' mistakes, we are to show boundaries of where they are.' = draw the official record of what POTA numbered (the easement's outline is Buena Vista's outline). Same spirit everywhere: never question POTA's park or move its pin; the 'POTA pin may be wrong' list is an INTERNAL check only (it tells us our outline might be wrong), never shown on a card as a correction of POTA.

- R-G7 GREY AREA G-ID3 (chat 2, Oct 29 3:55 PM, for Brandon): R-G7 step 5 says 'trace' the official line. A GIS layer (shapes with coordinates) can be traced exactly (R-G6, 3 m). A PICTURE map (a PDF / brochure with no coordinates, e.g. AZ State Parks' Dankworth Pond / River Island maps, CPW's Granada SWA map) can only be traced by placing it on the globe by hand: typically 10-50 m off, which breaks R-G6. Proposal: trace ONLY from official GIS layers or the agency's own parcel/ownership layer (4b); a picture map is used to CONFIRM which land and which pieces (and to pick the parcels), never as the line itself; a park with only a picture map and no layer or parcel = pin + Fable list. Pin until Brandon rules.
  - RULED Oct 29 3:57 PM (Brandon): 'put the link in the Fable file for each park you find like that for now... rather not have the rough big lines.' = NO line is ever traced from a picture map. Such a park stays a PIN; its picture-map link goes in its row in KPC_MissingBoundaries_ForFable_desk.md (WORK LOG BY STATE) marked 'picture map only (G-ID3): link kept, pin'. Lines only from official map data (GIS layers) or the agency's parcel / ownership layer.

**POTA'S RULES, PLAIN ANSWERS (HQ Oct 29 1:20 PM, Brandon: 'make a section for 2fer rules found at pota.app that makes it clear for 2fer, trails, river trails etc and anything else they may ask'). Quotes are from docs.pota.app/docs/rules.html (read again Oct 29 2026). Each answer says what POTA SAYS (quoted) and then OUR RULE (Brandon's rulings R0-R10 / Rule Sheet G, which only apply POTA's words). Anything not here = a numbered grey area for Brandon, not a guess.**
1. WHAT COUNTS AS A 2-FER? POTA: "Such a multi-park activation requires an overlapped area where all activated parks' boundaries intersect." "The intersection must entirely contain the activator and the station equipment." OURS: two POTA-NUMBERED parks whose OFFICIAL boundaries overlap on PUBLIC ground (R0, R3). Touching at a border = not a 2-fer. Land with no POTA number never pairs.
2. HOW MANY PARKS AT ONCE? POTA: "two-fer, three-fer, etc." with no limit; "A separate log must be submitted for each park." OURS: a 3-fer = one patch where all three overlap (R: 3-fers share one patch).
3. WHERE MUST THE OPERATOR BE? POTA: "The activator and all equipment must be within the park's boundary and on public property." Straddling a line = "not fully within the boundary and ... out of compliance." OURS: the whole station inside the overlap; the overlap patch we draw is that ground (50-yard patch rule).
4. WHICH MAP DECIDES? POTA: "Use maps provided by Parks on the Air as guidelines only. Refer to official park websites and agencies to find the official boundaries." OURS: the managing agency's own map (R-G1); PAD-US only where the agency has none; OpenStreetMap never. POTA pins can be wrong (Brandon 12:14).
5. PRIVATE LAND INSIDE A PARK? POTA: "Activators may not attempt to activate from private property, even if the private property is adjacent to, or surrounded by, park property." "Activators may not trespass on private property to access ... lands." OURS: private inholdings are cut out of the park's outline and labelled 'private land' (R3); a national forest = only the land the agency owns, never the proclamation line (R4).
6. A TRAIL THAT IS ITS OWN PARK (no land boundary)? POTA: "If a trail system or a river is designated as a Parks on the Air entity by itself (not as parts of a land park having a defined boundary), the activator and the station equipment must be on public property within 30.5 meters (100 feet) from the edge of the trail or river." OURS: the trail park = a 100 ft strip each side of the official trail line, PUBLIC ground only; where that strip overlaps another POTA park's boundary on public ground = 2-fer (TRAIL rows, e.g. Channahon x I&M Canal).
7. A RIVER THAT IS ITS OWN PARK (no land boundary)? Same POTA sentence. OURS: 100 ft from the EDGE OF THE WATER (Brandon Oct 28: 'river trails 100 ft from edge of water'), public ground only. A river park WITH a defined boundary (a Wild River corridor, e.g. Kentucky's Green River US-10105) uses its OFFICIAL CORRIDOR boundary, not the 100 ft rule (POTA: 'not as parts of a land park having a defined boundary'); that corridor overlapping another park = 2-fer (Mammoth Cave x Green River WR = YES).
8. A WATER TRAIL / BLUEWAY PARK? POTA has no separate rule: it is a 'river ... designated ... by itself' → 100 ft from the water's edge on public ground (OURS, Brandon Oct 28).
9. A TRAIL OR RIVER RUNNING THROUGH A LAND PARK? POTA: no explicit rule beyond #6/#7. OURS: if the trail/river is its OWN POTA park, its strip (or corridor) overlapping the land park on public ground = 2-fer; a trail that is just a feature inside the park is not a park (R0).
10. ON THE WATER (boat, lake, shoreline)? POTA: no rule for boats; only "Activations from vehicles, RVs, etc., parked on public property within the park's boundary are permissible" and "Aeronautical activations are permitted if the QSOs are made from airspace directly above the park." OURS: inside the official boundary = inside the park, water included; an overlap that is ONLY water is listed APART as 'water only' (R7), never hidden, never called land. A lake record joins a park only right at the pin (R-G4).
11. A PARK ON TWO STATES? POTA: a log "should be for only one park in a single location (in case the park straddles multiple locations)"; "Location (Chosen at time of upload if park crosses state lines)." OURS: one park, one outline; the state line is not a park boundary; a 2-fer is judged the same on either side.
12. CAN A PARK BE INSIDE ANOTHER PARK OF THE SAME AGENCY? POTA: "POTA does not create sub-parks within existing parks if both parks are part of the same government tier or agency." OURS: so two POTA numbers of the SAME agency on the same land = 'same land, two park numbers', shown with that label (R: same land); a state park sitting on federal-OWNED land = 2-fer in the overlap (R4, R-SF); a state-OWNED park inside a federal outer line = hard boundary, not a 2-fer.
13. IS THE PARK OPEN? POTA: "A park is considered open when the public has civil and legal access, or a special permission/permit ... is obtainable." "A park is closed when the governing agency/website clearly says that public access is prohibited or when the park itself ceases to exist." Seasonal closures of parts "may still allow lawful access to the remainder." "If in doubt, please call the park office/administrator." OURS: a closure is a closure only when the agency's own words close land to the public (Brandon Oct 29 9:23: quota hunts = notes; 'Tract 1 is closed to public access' = a real closure of that part); hunting-season rules and bag limits are never closures.
14. WHICH PARKS EXIST? POTA: "All new parks must be owned and operated by the two (2) highest tiers of government"; parks with "a formal government website including a boundary map SHALL" have it on their POTA page. OURS: we never add or question a park (P0, R0); if POTA numbers it, we find its official boundary (R-G1..G7) and apply the rules above.
15. WHOSE RULES WIN? POTA: local/federal laws and licence conditions "supersede anything contained in these documents"; POTA is "a self-regulated, community-monitored program." OURS: honor system; every card carries the 2-FER notice ('Parks' boundaries may overlap here, but we can't confirm it ...') and the maps notice (notes/notices.md). We never say parks DO overlap.
16. PARKING LOTS, ROADS, PULL-OFFS (Brandon Oct 29 1:20 PM: 'Parking lots are ok if your on the property it is ok and publicly open'): a parking lot, road or pull-off INSIDE the park's official boundary, on the park's property and open to the public = inside the park (POTA: "Activations from vehicles, RVs, etc., parked on public property within the park's boundary are permissible"). A lot on a public road right-of-way or private land outside the line = not in the park; our access points say 'Nearby public access, not the park's own' for those. A lot inside the OVERLAP of two parks = a 2-fer spot like any other ground.
17. NOT ANSWERED BY POTA (ask Brandon as a numbered grey area, keep the row NOT SURE meanwhile): a trail park's strip where the official trail line itself is missing; county or city parks POTA has numbered that have no official map.

**R-G8 'WORDS' PIECES (HQ ruling Oct 29 2:45 PM; Brandon 2:40 PM 'look at it make your ruling'; from chat 8's KPC_RuleG_KeyCheck_desk.md: Sequoia NP picked up Giant Sequoia National MONUMENT, Clearwater NF picked up Nezperce NF, Revheim Bay SRA picked up Indian Creek RA, Kodiak NWR picked up 'Kodiak Office').** Chat 8's three conditions are ADOPTED, with two clarifications:
- A piece matched only by SHARED WORDS (not the park's full name, an agreed alias, or the agency's own ID for that park: those are R-G1/R-G2/G-1 joins and are untouched) may be JOINED only if ALL THREE hold: (1) it shares a PLACE word with the park: a word that is not a type word (Park, Forest, Monument, Refuge, Recreation Area, Lake, Creek, Bay, State, National, Wildlife, Management, Area, Office…); (2) the KIND of place agrees (Park ≠ Monument, Forest ≠ Refuge, a WMA ≠ a bird refuge, an office is never park land); (3) it is not BIGGER than the park's own name-matched record once that is joined.
- A 'words' piece that fails any one = CHECK, never joined and never silently dropped: it goes to the waiting room for chat 11's yes/no + chat 2's recheck (G-4), so Mobile-Tensaw x W. L. Holland (lean yes) gets its answer there.
- Unaffected: R-G5 named units of a split park (Whitewater WMA Main Branch Unit), wetland districts and other tracts joined by the agency's own ID (G-1), and Ohio's short codes that spell the park (PASS).
Chat 2 builds it; chat 8 re-runs its key check after; a new grey area = a numbered rule for Brandon.

**RULE SHEET G, FABLE'S METHOD NOTES (Fable in HQ's seat, Oct 29 6:20 PM; Brandon 6:14 PM: 'organize the chat command them to do you method write the notes in the rule book'). Additions R-G9..R-G11 + process P-1..P-5. Nothing above changes; these sit inside Rule G.**
- **R-G9 PICTURE MAPS (replaces G-ID3's 'never', Brandon's 6:14 PM GO on Fable's terms; he can veto):** a line from a picture map (PDF / brochure / JPG with no coordinates) is NEVER Official and NEVER used by the 2-fer line or counted as a boundary. It MAY be drawn as grade **APPROXIMATE (picture map)** only when ALL hold: (1) the picture is the managing agency's own official map of THAT park; (2) it can be aligned to at least 3 fixed points that also exist on an official basemap (road junctions, a shoreline, a section corner), so the fit error is known; (3) the fit error is under 50 m; (4) it is labelled on every tool as 'approximate, from the agency's picture map, fit ±N m' and the notice line shows. Fails any one = pin + the link in the Fable file (G-ID3 as before). Built as one tool by chat 10 after the key release; until it exists, nothing is traced from pictures.
- **R-G10 'WHY NOT' TAG:** every park with no Official outline carries ONE tag in the key and in the Fable file row: `no-layer` (agency publishes nothing) / `picture-map-only` / `private` (the ground is not public) / `name-mismatch` (a record exists, the name does not agree: R-G2/R-G8) / `needs-parcel` (no record at the pin: R-G7 step 4b by county parcel) / `pin-doubt` (pin well outside the only candidate). The leftover list sorts itself by tag; the final review reads tags, not rows.
- **R-G11 PIN OUTSIDE THE OUTLINE (chat 11's 198):** a pin outside an outline is NOT a hold by itself. (a) Outline joined by the agency's own ID or the park's full name / agreed alias (R-G1+G-1) → RELEASE; the park goes on the 'POTA pin may be wrong' note (pins never move). (b) Outline joined by shared words (R-G8) or by location only → HELD for chat 11's yes/no. Chat 2 splits the 198 by join type; chat 11 looks only at (b).
- **P-1 SIZE UNITS FIRST:** state-layer ('sg') pieces carry square metres / feet labelled as acres; R-G8 (3) and every size check read that field. Fix: convert at the source once; sanity check every piece: stored acres vs its own polygon's computed area, flag any 10x off; rebuild; chat 8 re-confirms R-G8 and chat 11 re-sweeps sizes on THAT rebuild. The count drop after the 204 YES (11,312 → 11,308) is the same bug: explain it with the rebuild.
- **P-2 POLYGONS BEFORE PAGES:** for every open park, the look-up bot first runs point-in-polygon at the pin against EVERY layer we already know for that state (state-gis.json + national layers), one request per state. Chats 3 / 8 read only the hits. Web pages are for parks with no hit. The 76 open key pieces get the same layer probe in one batch (chat 9) before any more page reading.
- **P-3 ADDRESS + PARCEL = ONE JOB, BY COUNTY:** the 293 parks with no PAD-US record at the pin have one path left, R-G7 step 4b (county parcel map → owner). The address job gives the county. So: address → county → ONE `parcel` look-up request per county (points = every open park in it) → owner + parcel id into the Fable file. Never per park.
- **P-4 BATCH BY AGENCY:** parks of one agency (30 Oklahoma historic sites, a state's fishing lakes) share one layer and one page pattern: one probe / one request per agency, not per park.
- **P-5 CITY / COUNTY PARKS:** their official layers are county / city GIS (not state): a Source Book item per county that has POTA-numbered local parks; until read, tag `no-layer`.

**RULE SHEET G, CANADA (Fable, Oct 29 7:05 PM; Brandon 6:56 PM 'write new g code for Canada and give them a list of map layers'). Rule G (R-G1..R-G11, P-1..P-5) applies to Canada unchanged; these R-GC rules only say how it reads north of the border. The Canada key code = kpc_ca_key.py + the layer list in kpc_canada_bounds.py (EXTRA + BC WFS).**
- **R-GC1 OFFICIAL, BY TIER:** federal = Parks Canada's own layers (parks, NMCAs, administered lands) and ECCC (National Wildlife Areas, Migratory Bird Sanctuaries); provincial / territorial = the province's parks, protected-areas, Crown-land and wildlife layers; regional / municipal parks and Ontario conservation-authority lands = THAT body's own open-data layer (it is the managing agency; POTA numbered the park, so we honour it, R0). CPCAD (Canada's PAD-US) is the fallback ONLY where the agency publishes nothing. Never OSM.
- **R-GC2 TWO LANGUAGES, ONE NAME:** names match with accents and generic words dropped in BOTH languages (parc / park, réserve / reserve, lac / lake, rivière / river, île / island, mont / mount, pointe / point, baie / bay, Saint / St.). Designation words must agree ACROSS languages: parc national du Québec = provincial park (SÉPAQ), réserve écologique = ecological reserve, réserve faunique / ZEC = wildlife reserve (not a park), refuge d'oiseaux migrateurs = migratory bird sanctuary, aire protégée = protected area, site patrimonial = heritage site, lieu historique national = National Historic Site. Upper / Lower / Haut / Bas and Nord / Sud / Est / Ouest tell two halves apart (as today).
- **R-GC3 NATIONAL HISTORIC SITES (392 of the 1,296 'nothing near'):** the Directory of Federal Heritage Designations and Parks Canada 'Places' give POINTS, not boundaries. An NHS gets an outline only from (a) Parks Canada administered-lands / Canada Lands Administrative Boundaries polygons (sites Parks Canada runs), (b) a provincial heritage-property polygon, or (c) a municipal parcel naming the owner (R-G7 4b). Otherwise tag `no-layer` and stay a pin: most NHS are a building on a town lot and that is correct.
- **R-GC4 REGIONAL / MUNICIPAL PARKS (QC MRC 'parcs régionaux', BC regional districts, ON conservation authorities, city parks):** official = the MRC / regional district / authority / city open-data layer. One probe per body (P-4), tag `no-layer` until read (P-5). A Québec 'aire faunique communautaire' or 'aire de concentration d'oiseaux' under a park's pin is a WILDLIFE HABITAT designation, not a park: it never becomes the park's outline (R-G2 kind of place).
- **R-GC5 WATER:** Great Lakes / St. Lawrence / coastal NMCAs and marine reserves are water-only parks: outline from the agency layer, listed APART (R7), never land. A lake record joins only at the pin (R-G4).
- **R-GC6 CROWN LAND IS NOT A PARK:** a Crown-land polygon joins only when the agency's record names the park (a provincial park inside Crown land) or as the OWNER for R-G7 step 4b; the generic Crown block never becomes an outline.
- **R-GC7 NEW BRUNSWICK ZONES:** the PNA / wildlife layers carry one record per ZONE; a park = every zone of its name (R-G5 units), read by zone number as chat 2 built it; the 202 NB 'pin inside' parks are mostly this.
- **R-GC8 SAME GRADES, TAGS, PINS:** Official / Approximate / Pin only; R-G10 why-not tags (`no-layer` / `picture-map-only` / `private` / `name-mismatch` / `needs-parcel` / `pin-doubt`) in data/ca-key/why-not.json; POTA pins never move; 3 m lines (R-G6, Canada at ~5 m today → 3 m on the next re-read); R-G9 picture maps = Approximate only, tool first.
- **ORDER (Brandon 5:28 PM):** Canada layer PROBING + WIRING into the key is prep and may run now; R-G7 hand tracing in Canada waits until the US Fable book is through the Opus and Fable passes.

Official government sources only; open.canada.ca / provincial portals first (RESEARCH ORDER).**
